Concurrent workers explain the distinct stopping points; captures are not coherent whole-memory snapshots.

**Zoom-out decision: implement the selected QoL client's preferred native OpenAL route before expanding Miles further.** The client already tries `openal32.dll` then `soft_oal.dll` through SDL_LoadObject. Our `guest_graphics.c` loader currently recognizes only GL/GLU, so both genuinely fail and the game falls back to Miles. A native OpenAL provider can reuse an established positional mixer rather than separately implementing Miles/DirectSound spatialization and remaining MMX mixing.

- Downloaded and pinned **OpenAL Soft 1.24.3**, commit `dc7d7054a5b4f3bec1dc23a42fd616a0847af948`, in `ref/openal-soft`; recorded it in dependencies.lock.json and retained license notices. `scripts/build-native-openal.py` reproducibly builds an ARM64 static library with **required CoreAudio**. Upstream CMake explicitly supports CoreAudio on iOS; mobile has not been built/tested here.
- `scripts/test-native-openal.py` passes real CoreAudio playback/cursor advancement (**sample512**) and native loopback positional PCM: left/right movement reverses channel balance and distance reduces energy from **0.47750632 to0.00746104**. Initial test used the wrong backend selector `coreaudio`; corrected it to upstream's actual `core` selector and reran successfully. No null/Wave output fallback is permitted by the test. **This library is not yet connected to the original game.**
- Extracted 77 original IFC22 AL/ALC API-name candidates (including EFX and optional extensions). These are strings, not proof that all are mandatory or called. Verify the original resolver/consumers when building the finite adapter.

Primary-monitor selection and ANSI/Unicode monitor-information services now expose the configured Apple desktop. Monitor tokens occupy `0xc4000001..0xc4ffffff`, separate from desktop windows and DCs. The reached scheduling APIs are implemented in `guest_scheduling.c` for the current-process and current-thread pseudo handles. Realtime/background classes remain unsupported. `GetProcessAffinityMask` reports the existing unrestricted guest group, capped at 32 online native CPUs. `SetThreadAffinityMask` validates its mask and returns `ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`: Apple cache-affinity hints cannot implement strict Windows CPU binding, and no fictional pinning state is stored.

The emitted ABI passes checked, ordinary and ThreadSanitizer macOS tests and the sole iPad Simulator component. The three imported SDL3 feature queries now use the existing x86 guest CPUID profile: `SDL_HasMMX` returns true, `SDL_HasSSE` and `SDL_HasAVX` return false. They do not report ARM host instructions. The read-only CPU hardware registry is now implemented through `RegOpenKeyExW`, `RegQueryValueExW` and `RegCloseKey`. The finite HKLM hardware tree uses the guest processor count; UTF-16 key/value names are case-insensitive in the supported ASCII namespace. Each key handle carries an address-space identity and access rights, is separately closable, and is never reused. Registry tokens use `0xe5000001..0xe5ffffff`; review caught and corrected an initial overlap with process-heap tokens before accepting integration. Software/profile registry persistence remains unimplemented and requests stop explicitly.

The unavailable-frequency policy is now explicit in `config/runtime/cpu-metadata.json`: native `hw.cpufrequency` is used when valid; `ENOENT`, an empty result, or zero use 2400 MHz compatibility metadata. This follows the pinned libuv Darwin implementation and is **not a physical clock measurement**. Other syscall errors and malformed sizes remain errors. The native brand and processor count remain host-backed. The fallback passes the existing checked/ordinary/ThreadSanitizer registry tests and the sole iPad Simulator component; eleven injected syscall outcomes additionally verify fallback, native values, malformed results and error preservation. All three Apple targets compile. The full checked native continuation passes this startup boundary.

The allocator and selected callback/API bridges are now bound. Remaining checked 32-bit vararg/ownership conversions include: native long/pointer widths differ, public CURLMsg/slist layouts require conversion, returned URL strings transfer ownership, and the original Windows-style event service must drive the translated native sockets. The actual original socket callback at0x100217c0 uses CURLINFO_PRIVATE and curl_multi_assign and registers the descriptor with its event service; the timer callback at0x10021710 can reenter curl_multi_socket_action. Do not hand guest pointers to uninstrumented curl/TLS or bypass callback reentry, socket lifetime or memory checks. Frozen dependency sources, static archives, native test executable, all three linked targets and reports are in `generated/integration-review/http-backend-checkpoint-20261002`. That earlier dependency checkpoint alone does not establish guest integration or gameplay.

`guest_curl_allocator.c` now implements the allocation boundary. Each real guest allocation has a private host mirror; uninstrumented curl receives only the host buffer. Checked copies publish live buffers at API and allocator boundaries. On return from guest code, opaque live bytes must still match the host buffer: guest mutations are rejected, never imported into native pointer-bearing structures. New calloc/strdup results and preserved realloc prefixes also validate their allocator contracts. Every supported malloc/free/realloc/strdup/calloc invokes a finite cdecl guest callback, including NULL free. The selected original realloc's NULL result retains the old allocation even for size zero; static inspection of0x100ae0b0 confirms that contract. String duplication stages short input below the guest stack and large input through a temporary guest allocation, while the registered strdup owns the result. Exporting a returned string removes its mirror and transfers its actual guest allocation to the caller. Nested API frames require explicit guest-callback suspension/resumption; unframed, cross-thread and allocator-recursive calls stop. This is an owner-thread implementation matching the synchronous-resolver candidate, not general threaded curl support. Mirrored opaque storage does not translate public curl structures or expose native pointers as guest pointers.

The four real HTTP/TLS transfers and their error cases pass through emitted cdecl guest allocator fixtures in both normal and compiler-guarded builds. The newer startup checkpoint above separately establishes original allocator-backed multi initialization. Original game HTTP/socket/timer execution remains unfinished; the newer component evidence above uses emitted callback fixtures.

## Implementation history and supporting evidence

- Acquired and SHA-256-pinned the official 539 MiB base installer, QoL preview 20250922, RC2a diagnostic installer, and PlayT2's linked HD 2.0 archive. `inputs-manifest.json` contains the identities, URLs, versions and static module dependencies. Authenticode presence is recorded separately from signature verification; certificate validation has not been completed.
- Built REWise locally and extracted the real base executable, which reports version `0, 25034, 0, 0`, at guest image base `0x400000`. Created separate `ref/control` and `ref/product` installations. Product includes the chosen QoL modules and texture archive.
- Pinned HaloPad at `c4aad6873057e48cb7e6aca907797722ef7da5b9` and extracted its tracked reference files into this project's ignored `ref/halopad`. HaloPad's active checkout was left untouched.
- Audited Tribes2.exe: 953,370 decoded instructions, 21,523 candidate functions and 107,065 unclassified bytes. Audited IFC22: 357,376 decoded instructions, 3,905 candidate functions and 31,272 unclassified bytes. These are static analysis counts, not proof of runtime coverage.
- Generated native ARM64 implementations of the original CRC32 routine (`0x6c32f0`) and version getter (`0x6949c0`). All 244 original-x86 versus ARM64 cases pass, checking registers, flags, stack bytes, input preservation and independent CRC results. The generated game-derived C stays ignored.
- Built a separate Tribes-patched SRW from hash-verified cached sources, preserving the original tools and HaloPad checkout. Added 22 MMX packed operations, MOVD, EMMS, SETAE and JCXZ. Native arithmetic passes 7,936 x86 differential cases. All 671 actual MMX sites pass 8,052 emitted-LLVM-to-ARM64 cases checking GPRs, flags, physical MMX/x87 alias storage and memory. All six actual SETAE/JCXZ sites pass 2,304 cases. Helpers compile for iOS devices and the simulator. The emitted-code tests caught and corrected temporary-register loss across helper calls; a single-call lowering now preserves whole values. Full x87 tag/exception integration remains pending. These are individual instruction tests, not a game-session acceptance result.
- Implemented the eight rejected relocated-immediate ADD forms and both REPNE SCASW sites. All 3,764 actual emitted ARM64 cases pass, including arithmetic flags, unaligned memory, both scan directions, match/exhaustion and zero count with an unmapped pointer.
- Implemented FTST and replaced inherited FCLEX/FFREE no-ops with status/tag updates. All 34 actual FTST sites pass 17,306 emitted ARM64 cases against x86 through Rosetta, with additional raw extended80 classification and native stack/tag transition checks. Tag tracking now covers FNINIT, pushes, pops, EMMS, FFREE and state save/restore paths, and basic clears preserve sticky exceptions. The legacy reference runtime stores binary64; the new candidate uses raw extended80 stack values. Full tag/exception dispatch, runtime coverage and engine selection remain gates. Unserviced exceptions fail closed.
- Implemented conservative SHL, SHR, SAR, ROL and SHRD lowering. Pure arithmetic passes 133,120 x86 differential cases across every CL count and supported width. All 5,477 actual game sites pass 60,247 emitted ARM64 checks. Nineteen selected producer/branch pairs spanning all eleven formerly rejected conditional boundaries pass 2,736 checks. Architecturally undefined flag bits are excluded; full intervening paths and whole-engine execution remain unverified.
- Generated the complete conservative diagnostic image as 434 MiB of LLVM IR. Windows decorated imports now use correctly quoted LLVM identifiers with their original symbol names preserved. The same ARM64 compiler completed after its launcher observation timeout, producing a verified 55,281,432-byte Mach-O ARM64 object with 188,413 text symbols and 401 unresolved symbols. The orphan compiler exit status is unavailable; object identity, content hash and symbol inventory were checked independently. The compile driver now records observation intervals without killing a live compiler, and recovery metadata prevents duplicate work. The diagnostic retains three unsupported-instruction traps and has no imported API implementations.
- Downloaded and pinned Berkeley SoftFloat 3e for an extended80 arithmetic foundation. Its 8086 specialization builds natively for macOS, iOS device and simulator, with thread-local arithmetic settings. A pure adapter passes 18,000 raw x87 comparisons across all rounding modes/precision controls and 40,000 concurrent operations with configuration restoration. The candidate stack now uses raw extended80 storage and native math; noncanonical/exceptional cases, stack faults, tags and exception delivery still require validation.
- Found and documented oracle limitations: Unicorn omits FTST NaN exception flags; Rosetta does not implement the MMX/x87 tag alias transition or reliable unmasked exception behavior. The tests exclude those unsupported oracle claims and use Intel's pinned SDM for raw exceptional classifications. No whole-game floating-point correctness claim follows from these instruction tests.
- Added a separate native extended80 stack runtime with the existing SR helper ABI and preserved CPU prefix offsets. It now provides all 82 x87 helper symbols required by the strict object, including the eight remainder/scaling/transcendental operations. Register arithmetic (including destination-register and pop forms) passes 46,800 raw x86 comparisons across all precision/rounding modes, now including C1 rounding-direction flags. FBLD/FBSTP/FXTRACT pass 1,528 actual emitted ARM64 cases, including selected exceptional formats, pseudo-denormals and decimal C1 rounding. Existing FTST and MMX regressions pass after the CPU layout extension. Memory/integer helpers, stack faults, transcendental exception status, save/restore and unmasked exception delivery still need coverage; no whole-game runtime has executed.
- Implemented ordered and unordered comparisons with separate helpers, preserving sticky exception flags. All 6,144 raw comparisons pass. Fixed inherited FCOM macros that incorrectly called FUCOM, and corrected missing pseudo-denormal DE classification against Intel SDM page 221 (Volume 1 section 8.2.2). The new strict object's IR predates the FCOM macro correction and must be regenerated for runtime acceptance; this is recorded in its runtime follow-up metadata.
- Downloaded and hash-pinned Bochs pure floating math sources at `93a047cc3554a1650c12e0aafbe105e7d6143076` to evaluate the remaining eight scaling/remainder/transcendental helpers. Its CPU interpreter and instruction engine are excluded. All 227 pure-math translation units and a native C ABI bridge build successfully. The candidate and its extended80 stack compile for macOS, iOS device and simulator. FPREM/FSCALE match all 1,056 sampled x86 value/status cases. The six transcendental operations pass 1,480 included high-precision numeric-bound checks, with explicit range/domain exclusions; this is sampled evidence, not a global accuracy or exception-status proof. All 649 original sites pass 10,384 emitted ARM64 ABI checks. Rosetta differs on some transcendental status bits and small numeric results; exception-status acceptance remains open.
- Prepared the original guest-address model for the strict engine: 32,419 indirect transfers use native finite dispatch. Actual SRW CRC/version blocks pass 244 original-x86 comparisons through this address model, including original return addresses, complete stack bytes, registers and defined flags. The original PE pages remain data-only. The complete address-model image compiled successfully into a 43,368,448-byte native ARM64 object (Clang exit 0). All 298 import symbols use their correct, sanitized binding identities, including Winsock ordinals. A linked diagnostic enters the actual CRT at `0x6fde60`. After implementing exception-filter registration, it now passes six critical-section initializations, locking/unlocking and a real 65,544-byte fixed guest allocation, then standard-handle discovery and three same-process native descriptor duplications, then environment snapshots, TLS, command-line retrieval and native performance timing, reaching `GetDiskFreeSpaceA`. Registers, defined flags, the exception-list head, registered guest handler, the entire 2 MiB stack and all loaded PE image data match original x86 execution with a single-thread service contract model at that boundary. This is not a comparison against executing Windows DLLs. Unimplemented Windows APIs stop explicitly. Exception delivery, complete startup, runtime services and callbacks remain unfinished.
- Implemented native recursive critical sections using pthread mutexes with lifetime/ownership tracking and isolated guest-image keys. Tests pass recursive ownership, contended try/wait, 160,000 protected updates across eight threads, 3,000 delete/reinitialize lifetimes and independent guest windows. Added fixed guest-address `GlobalAlloc`/`GlobalFree`/`GlobalSize`, 8-byte-or-better alignment, reusable/coalesced extents, zero initialization, overflow/OOM handling and thread-local last errors. Tests pass 8,000 concurrent allocation lifetimes, zero/reuse, invalid frees and last-error isolation. Both test suites pass ThreadSanitizer without reports. Movable global-memory handles, reallocation and full virtual-memory APIs remain open. New service objects and generated stdcall wrappers compile for iOS device and simulator; those new bindings have not executed on a physical device or simulator.
- Added native descriptor-backed handle registration, standard-handle discovery/redirection, character/disk/pipe/socket classification, safe borrows and closes, the current-process pseudo handle and same-process same-access descriptor duplication. Standard handles duplicate host descriptors without closing the application’s originals; borrows survive concurrent guest closes. Handle IDs are original 32-bit guest values, not host pointers. Tests cover absent/redirected handles, invalid handles, shared file offsets, source-close duplication and 8,000 concurrent lifetimes, passing normal and ThreadSanitizer builds. Device and simulator service/wrapper object builds pass. Process/thread object duplication, reduced-access duplication, child-process inheritance and file/console I/O remain unverified or unimplemented.
- Added configured OEM environment snapshots with matching free/lifetime checks, immutable ANSI command-line storage, native thread-local slots with generation-based reuse, and native monotonic performance timing. Tests cover 8,000 concurrent environment snapshots, 120,000 isolated TLS set/get cycles, live-thread reuse, slot exhaustion and 10,000 monotonic samples. New services compile for iOS device/simulator; runtime tests are macOS only. The environment profile names planned Game/Temp mounts, which are not yet implemented.
- Fixed indirect-dispatch scratch writes that overwrote guest-stack bytes during longer startup. A per-CPU mailbox now carries indirect targets; the complete ARM64 image was regenerated and compiled successfully. The 244 original CRC/version cases plus an independent x86 indirect-jump fixture pass with complete stack preservation. Added per-CPU 64-bit x87 result buffers in a separate, nonexecutable guest arena, tested across 16,000 concurrent result copies. The bootstrap x86 reference now explicitly starts x87 with control word `0x037f` and empty tags, matching native initialization; its former Unicorn defaults were not the expected thread state. The longer comparison uses native clock samples as replayed external inputs and reaches the disk-space boundary with identical registers, defined flags, full stack and image data. These remain bootstrap evidence, not menus or gameplay.
- Built and cold-launched the native macOS diagnostic. Built and launched the iPad Pro 13-inch (M5) simulator, shut it down, then tested iPhone 17 Pro. Both display build 25034 and CRC `cbf43926`. Compiled an iOS device bundle; it still needs development signing and physical-device testing.
- Verified touch jet and fire events on both simulators, and nonzero movement on iPad. Fixed loss of final look deltas on short fire drags and verified the resulting iPad and iPhone event logs. Input tests cover simultaneous ski/jet/fire, independent sources, radial normalization, opposing-key handling, look consumption, normal release and cancellation. Controller and physical keyboard/mouse behavior still need device verification.
- Native BSD-socket probes received valid Tribes 2 UDP responses from **DAD TRIBES** and **Legacy CTF+** on this Mac, and from both iPad and iPhone simulators using live endpoint discovery. Both replies reported query version 5, protocol 52, minimum protocol 51 and build 25034. Their minimum protocol strengthens the need to preserve the selected community patch behavior; a stock-client fallback cannot be assumed compatible. This is reachability evidence only: no authentication or game session was attempted successfully.
- Checked HD archive CRCs and paths: 1,487 PNGs, maximum dimension 1,280; seven images exceed 512 pixels. No scripts or executable modules occur in the HD archive. The catalog calls the selected pack reworked, while its `Info.txt` says “Upscaled and processed Textures”; both facts are retained rather than assuming different archive contents. GPU upload and visual comparisons remain unverified.

- Added native filesystem mounts and disk queries, boot-millisecond and UTC clocks. Eight service test groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer runs, including 8,000 concurrent path lookups and 10,000 clock samples. All updated services compile for macOS, iOS device and simulator. Full native startup matches original x86 under the declared service model at `GetStartupInfoA`, across all GPRs, the compared flags, FS head, the full 2 MiB stack and loaded PE image. The reference executes about 7.5 million instructions with a 10 million instruction budget; live disk/time samples are shared inputs and tested independently. An intermediate stop exposed undefined IMUL flags, correctly excluded using Intel rather than Capstone's incorrect SF classification. These are initialization diagnostics, not rendering or playable-client acceptance.

- Added immutable launch metadata, main-image handle/path queries, actual memory DC/bitmap objects and window-class registration with finite ARM64 callback validation. Twelve native service test groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer runs; the new tests include 16,000 concurrent launch reads, 16,000 module lookups, 8,000 GDI lifetimes and 8,000 class lifetimes. Native resource tests check selected-bitmap ownership, stale handles, creating-thread exit, path truncation, duplicate classes and prevention of unregistering a class retained by a window. Updated services compile for all three Apple targets. The full engine passes the original-x86 service-model comparison at `CreateWindowExA`, after about 7.5 million instructions, including registers, compared flags, FS head, complete stack and loaded PE data. A bootstrap handle-selector bug was caught by this comparison and fixed; LLVM wrappers now declare native void helpers correctly. No actual WinSock window, messages, renderer or game session is established.

- Added CPU-aware stdcall wrappers and a finite AOT callback bridge: callbacks use protected register copies, shared x87/MMX thread state and bounded guest stack frames. A real hidden notification-window registry retains classes and handles synchronous creation/destruction and rejection cleanup. Tests cover nested native fastcc callbacks and 8,000 concurrent window lifetimes. Added Winsock 1.1 startup/cleanup and native IPv4 socket, bind, getsockname, ioctl, sendto and recvfrom services. A local native peer exchanges real UDP in both directions through the guest ABI; nonblocking reads, peeking, zero datagrams, truncation, final cleanup and 8,000 concurrent socket lifetimes pass. Datagram truncation uses recvmsg output flags correctly on Darwin. Fourteen service groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer runs, and current bindings compile for macOS, device and simulator. The full native engine and original x86 agree at the first CPUID instruction, including all registers, compared flags (now also IF/ID), complete stack, loaded PE data and the three window callbacks. The diagnostic runner now captures unsupported hardware helpers; its reference budget is 30 million instructions because initialization exceeded 10 million. This is not an authenticated match or a renderer acceptance result.

## Current engine gates

The first whole-image SRW run failed on an instruction containing two copies of the same address. Four ambiguous fixups now remain literal in the original fixed guest-address model. The initial diagnostic run exposed **716 unsupported instruction sites**. Tribes-local translations now reduce the missing-opcode census to **zero sites**. FBLD, FXTRACT and FBSTP now lower into native extended80 helpers. The eight relocated-immediate ADD forms are implemented; optimized mode still has 13 incoming-flag region issues (including the two word scans' direction flag). Strict optimized translation stops at `0x48fbaf`. The conservative mode retains arithmetic flags and carries incoming CPU state across regions. Its previous **1,312 missing flag updates are implemented and tested**; the new census reports zero uncalculated flags and zero unhandled operand forms. Strict conservative whole-image SRW translation now succeeds with zero missing opcodes, zero uncalculated flags and zero unhandled forms. The fresh strict translation compiled successfully (Clang exit 0) into a verified 55,281,464-byte ARM64 Mach-O object with 188,413 text symbols, 403 unresolved symbols and no unsupported-instruction trap import. This closes the translator instruction census, not runtime or game-session acceptance. The previously verified object remains a historical diagnostic with three traps.

IFC22's static audit identifies **432 direct writes into the game's code**, 925 guest-code immediate candidates, and 46 patch/loader boundary call sites. Indirect writes, runtime trampolines, callbacks and patch timing remain unresolved. Simply translating the original unpatched image and loading this DLL cannot satisfy the target. A separate IFC22 translation attempt stops in the inherited decoder at `0x1000c70b`, on a VEX-encoded AVX instruction. The decoder must gain this support or be replaced through a validated Tribes-specific path before this module can be translated.

The actual Mac reference has not reached verified menus or online gameplay. CrossOver's installed GUI reports an expired trial. Launches through its CLI and the available Whisky Wine 7.7 runtime loaded the executable/patch and showed native host activity, but no successful reference session, screenshot, map or login was established. These attempts are failures to meet the reference gate, not proof that Tribes 2 universally fails on macOS.

An earlier initialization checkpoint stopped at `GetKeyState`: virtual key 20 (Caps Lock). The current boundary is recorded at the top of this document. The original game now completes 104 MapVirtualKeyA calls and 312 ToAscii translations, building its full 1,548-byte text-input cache. Both original device-enumeration callbacks now return. Keyboard and pointer formats, 128-slot buffers and pointer relative-axis mode are configured. Native formats retain 256 keyboard entries in a 1,024-byte packet and 11 pointer entries in a 44-byte packet. Device creation, the original v2-interface query, capabilities and all 256 keyboard object callbacks now complete. The generated format has unique IDs/offsets and the expected key GUID for every entry. Original keyboard enumeration (type 3, attached-only flags 1) now enters actual callback `0x556f90` and the original constructor then creates and queries its native keyboard device and enumerates 256 keyboard scan-code slots (113 have specific labels) through original callback `0x557f50`. Both outer device-enumeration callbacks complete in the current startup comparison. The engine now loads a finite native DirectInput provider, resolves `DirectInputCreateA` and creates its version 5 root COM object. Native tokens and exact 32-bit vtable memory keep all calls in precompiled ARM64 wrappers; this does not load or execute the original Windows DInput DLL. The four original CPUID instructions now run with a fixed guest profile advertising tested x87/MMX only, and the original detection selects flags 7. Both original GetVersionExA calls complete with independently checked 156-byte extended and 148-byte legacy layouts. The hidden empty-title WinSock window now exists and executes its actual native window procedure at `0x559a90` for WM_NCCREATE, WM_NCCALCSIZE and WM_CREATE. The original x86 executes those same callbacks in the differential comparison. Native Winsock 1.1 negotiation and current-directory queries then complete. Visible Apple windows, message queues, async socket notifications and native DirectInput integration remain unfinished. Launch metadata snapshots real standard handles; main-module metadata preserves guest handle `0x400000` and its full Windows path. The engine creates an actual memory DC and a 256×256 monochrome bitmap during font-cache initialization. These resources have real storage and tested lifetimes; fonts, drawing and renderer integration remain unfinished. Explicit native mounts now map C:\ root to an owned runtime directory, C:\TribesPad\Game to the downloaded product installation, and Temp to an owned runtime folder. Existing ASCII paths resolve with case-insensitive components, relative/dot normalization and symlink containment; UNC/device paths, full ANSI decoding, file creation and physical-device mounts remain unfinished. Real disk geometry and available blocks come from the mounted filesystem. GetTickCount uses native boot milliseconds, and GetSystemTimeAsFileTime supplies real UTC FILETIME. File/console reads and writes, pathname mapping and remaining CRT services are unfinished. OpenGL-to-Metal behavior, Miles/OpenAL audio, remaining Windows initialization, engine bindings, accounts and game-session networking remain unfinished. The diagnostic Apple shell has no whole-game renderer. No 60 FPS, 30-minute match, custom-map transition, physical device or multiplayer acceptance claim is made.

## Evidence

The tracked summary is `docs/progress-2026-09-30.json`. Full private artifacts remain ignored:

- `generated/inputs-audit.json`, `inputs-manifest.json`: actual inputs and module inventories.
- `generated/analysis/{tribes2,ifc22}`: recursive audits, instruction boundaries and relocation hints.
- `generated/qol-patch-audit.json`, `generated/texture-audit.json`: patch and texture evidence.
- `generated/srw/`, `generated/ifc22-lift.log`: whole-image translator attempts.
- `generated/slices/results.json`: 244-case oracle comparison.
- `generated/mmx/{results.json,lowering/results.json}`, `generated/conditionals/results.json`: packed semantics and actual emitted ARM64 instruction comparisons.
- `generated/runtime-lowering/results.json`, `generated/x87/results.json`: actual ADD/SCASW and FTST/status/tag comparisons.
- `generated/flags-gaps.json`: historical baseline of 1,312 flag-update gaps, now implemented.
- `generated/shift/{results.json,lowering/results.json}`, `generated/flags/seams/results.json`: shift semantics, actual sites and selected region seams.
- `generated/native-win32/`: service unit/ThreadSanitizer results and iOS-target object builds.
- `generated/softfloat/`: pinned extended80 libraries, adapter/runtime builds, helper coverage and differential/concurrency evidence.
- `generated/float80-strict-lift.log`, `generated/srw/run-20260930T101044-tribes2`: successful strict translation, verified native object and runtime follow-up metadata.
- `generated/bochs-math/`: pure math builds, accuracy bounds, x86 traces and emitted ABI results; pinned source hashes are in `dependencies.lock.json`.
- `generated/srw/run-20260930T101044-tribes2/va/`: original-address rewrite, native dispatch and actual CRC/version comparisons.
- `generated/srw/run-20260930T092611-tribes2/native-census/`: whole LLVM output, verified ARM64 object, symbol inventory and compiler recovery metadata.
- `generated/tools/SRW-tribes/tribes-build.json`: separate compiler identity; `scripts/patches/srw-tribes.patch` holds its tracked changes.
- `generated/validation/{ipad,iphone}`: simulator touch records, screenshots and iPad and iPhone server replies.
- `generated/apple/{macos,simulator,device}`: diagnostic bundles, build logs and binary identities.
- `generated/reference-launch.log`, `generated/whisky-*.log`, `generated/whisky-sample.txt`: unsuccessful desktop-reference attempts.

Current checkpoint: the full native engine and original x86 match after 17,463,222 reference instructions through CPU/version queries, native DirectInput root creation and the original keyboard callback/constructor, at GetKeyState. All 256 keyboard and 11 pointer object callbacks completed. All GPRs, defined arithmetic flags plus IF/ID, the complete stack, PE data, all 8,634,368 bytes of live guest heap and original hidden-window callbacks agree. Twenty native groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer tests; macOS, device and simulator object builds pass. The virtual CPU identity describes tested lowering rather than Apple hardware; the version identity describes the legacy guest API contract rather than the host OS. Neither establishes playable-game acceptance.

The DirectInput root has genuine allocated guest object/vtable memory and locked host reference metadata; QueryInterface preserves COM identity for IUnknown/ANSI v1/v2, and final Release reclaims its guest storage. Tests cover 8,000 concurrent object lifetimes, 32,000 shared-reference pairs, 64,000 provider load/export/release cycles and 128,000 concurrent calls through the real emitted LLVM fastcc/stdcall wrappers plus 96,000 concurrent two-argument enumeration callbacks. These verify exact stack consumption, canaries, all unrelated register/FP bytes and thread-local error preservation. Twenty groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer runs. Current macOS, device and simulator runtime objects compile. At the original keyboard enumeration entry, native and original x86 match registers, defined flags, full stack/image data and the DirectInput object/vtable bytes. Device state and acquisition still stop explicitly; range queries are implemented, while other properties remain gated; device creation/metadata and descriptor enumeration callbacks are implemented, while Apple event feed, device state/acquisition and game controls remain unfinished.

Native input enumeration now serializes original 560-byte DX3 and 580-byte DX5 descriptors from private host-owned catalog snapshots. Tests check descriptor identities/order, type and attachment filters, no-force-feedback filtering, early stop, caller register preservation, nested callbacks and catalog replacement inside a callback. The linked whole-engine bootstrap and original x86 agree through the original keyboard constructor and object enumeration at GetKeyState, including descriptor bytes, callback arguments, current callback registers, complete guest stack/PE data and root object/vtable memory. The native ABI test executable ran successfully in iPad Pro 13-inch (M5), then iPhone 17 Pro, with only one simulator booted at a time. This uses explicit software input proxies and does not verify UIKit events or playable controls.

Native DirectInput device objects now have the 27-slot ANSI v1/v2 vtable, their own guest storage, canonical interface identity and a retained parent interface. Final device Release also drops the parent reference safely. GetCapabilities supports guarded, unaligned 24/44-byte layouts and updates attachment flags from the current catalog. GetDeviceInfo supports 560/580-byte layouts from the creation snapshot. Tests cover 8,000 concurrent device lifetimes, parent release while devices remain alive, interface aliases, exact vtables, keyboard/pointer/controller capacity metadata, catalog removal/replacement, failure-output clearing and buffer/error preservation. All 20 groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer runs. Current runtime builds pass for macOS, device and simulator, and the updated ABI executable passes on iPhone then iPad, one booted simulator at a time. The full native engine and original x86 agree through the actual keyboard v2 query/capabilities and object-enumeration calls at GetKeyState, including the complete 112-byte device object/vtable. Polling, buffered events, acquisition, buffered events, other properties and Apple input feed remain unfinished.


Native object enumeration supplies guarded 292-byte DX3 and 316-byte DX5 records with GUIDs, IDs, offsets and names. The explicit software profile now has all 256 keyboard scan-code slots (113 specific labels), three relative pointer axes and eight pointer buttons. Tests cover class filters, early stop, descriptor usage fields, nested callbacks, immutable device snapshots across catalog replacement and CPU/stack preservation. These pass on macOS, iPad and iPhone; physical input and touch-to-engine state remain unfinished. The original game copies the descriptors into its control map; the expanded bootstrap compares every live guest heap allocation as well as the full stack and image. This exposed stale environment bytes after Darwin's lazy page discard. New allocations now explicitly zero their span, a valid deterministic policy even for flags 0; reuse tests cover both allocation modes. The updated whole-engine differential passes at MapVirtualKeyA after 17,463,222 x86 instructions. All 20 native groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer checks, and runtime objects compile for macOS, device and simulator.


The 256-entry format check corrected an earlier catalog mismatch: caps advertised 256 keyboard buttons while only 113 objects were enumerated. That left unused object descriptors in the game's format and triggered range-query fallbacks. The software keyboard now enumerates every byte-sized scan-code slot with deterministic names, including reserved slots; this does not assert that a physical keyboard has 256 keys. The original game constructs a fully populated 256-entry format and skips those fallback queries. Range property retrieval is independently implemented and tested through emitted ABI wrappers: guarded unaligned 24-byte structures, unchanged headers, relative signed-LONG wrap limits, explicit software absolute limits, ID/HID-usage selection, missing IDs, unsupported buttons/device-wide ranges and invalid inputs. Other property types remain strict gates; BYOFFSET range queries now resolve the retained caller format. All 20 groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer; runtime objects compile on all three Apple targets and the updated range/ABI tests run on iPhone then iPad, one booted at a time. MapVirtualKeyA is still an explicit diagnostic stop; no device acquisition or live game input is claimed.


SetDataFormat now resolves original object GUIDs, class/instance IDs, wildcard instances and optional unavailable objects into private host-owned mappings. It validates record/header sizes, packet bounds/alignment, overlapping data ranges and duplicate selections; failed replacements preserve the prior map, and later changes to guest GUID/format buffers cannot change it. Final device Release reclaims the map. BYOFFSET range queries use the retained format and distinguish missing entries from an unset format. Tests exercise these paths through the real emitted ABI wrappers across 8,000 concurrent device lifetimes, with normal/ThreadSanitizer results and sequential iPad/iPhone simulator execution. Runtime objects compile for all three Apple targets. A native format trace independently compares all 256 stored index/offset/width mappings to the original game's request. Full startup differential now passes at MapVirtualKeyA after 17,463,222 reference instructions. Live input, buffering, acquisition, rendered menus and online matches remain unverified.


Native buffer-size and axis-mode properties now validate guarded 20-byte structures, preserve failed-update buffers/state and allocate owned event-ring storage. Size 0 disables buffering, size 1 stores no events, oversized requests clamp to 4,096; the game's 128 slots hold at most 127 events. Axis mode defaults from the proxy and is updated by data format or explicit property, with invalid values rejected. The event ring preserves four-DWORD records and pending FIFO order, reports dropped incoming events, preserves overflow while peeking, and supports consume/discard/reset. Tests cover 16,000 independent ring lifetimes, 64,000 wrap/overflow cycles, zero/one sizes, clamping and guarded property get/set through 8,000 concurrent device lifetimes. Twenty groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer. Runtime objects compile for macOS/device/simulator; both the ABI and event-ring executables pass on iPhone then iPad with one booted simulator. Native property traces prove both allocated 128-slot/127-event buffers and pointer relative mode; both retained formats also match original requests. The completed keyboard/pointer callbacks now lead through MapVirtualKeyA through ToAscii to GetKeyState after 17,463,222 reference instructions with full heap/stack/image agreement. This does not yet implement GetDeviceData, acquisition, live event production or Apple event feed.


MapVirtualKeyA now uses an explicit fixed US software layout from `config/runtime/keyboard-layout.json`, with generated readonly tables checked before native builds/tests. Legacy modes 0..3 implement scan/VK/unshifted-character mapping, generic versus left/right modifiers, E0/E1-prefixed keys, and the navigation/numpad and Pause/NumLock ambiguities. Unmapped/reserved keys return zero; Vista-only mode 4 is outside the declared legacy contract. The layout is independent of Apple locale and does not establish non-US/IME support or actual Windows keyboard-DLL equivalence. Tests check known letters, modifiers, punctuation, function/media/navigation keys, invalid codes and 624,000 concurrent readonly mappings. Twenty groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer, all runtime objects compile for macOS/device/simulator, and the mapping executable passes on iPad then iPhone alongside existing ABI/ring tests, one booted simulator at a time. The first original scan-to-VK call returns Escape with full startup memory/register/flag agreement, reaching ToAscii after 17,463,222 reference instructions. Text tables, text entry, live device feed and gameplay remain unfinished.


ToAscii now uses explicit US character columns for Shift/Control states, Caps Lock XOR Shift on letters, control characters including NUL, punctuation, and keypad characters independent of Num/Scroll toggle bits. The US profile has no dead keys or ligatures; Ctrl+Alt has no AltGr translation. Each translated character writes one unaligned-safe WORD and preserves surrounding bytes and input state; noncharacters leave output unchanged. Null/live thread snapshots, Alt-only decimal composition and unknown flags still fail explicitly. Tests include 2,496,000 guarded concurrent character calls, shifted/caps combinations, controls, NUL, generic/extended modifiers and menu/key-release parameters. All 20 groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer; runtime objects compile for all Apple targets and updated keyboard tests pass on iPhone then iPad, one booted at a time. Original game construction completes 104 scan mappings and 312 character calls; its full 1,548-byte cache (53 nonzero rows) matches the original-x86 service model and has a recorded hash. Full memory/register/flag comparison passes at GetKeyState(Caps Lock) after 17,463,222 reference instructions. Keyboard-DLL equivalence, live processed key states, Apple text entry and playable controls remain unverified.


Processed keyboard state now has an immutable configured process seed and private state per owning thread. GetKeyState returns pressed/toggle bits; GetKeyboardState copies a guarded 256-byte snapshot. Retrieved-message transitions aggregate left/right modifiers and toggle Caps/Num/Scroll only on a new down edge, preserving toggles on release and autorepeat. Null-state ToAscii reads the same snapshot. Tests cover 640,000 concurrent transition cycles, fresh-thread cleanup/isolation, seed copying, invalid events, guarded unaligned snapshots and Caps/Shift/Control character changes. All 21 native groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer checks; runtime objects compile for macOS/device/simulator, and all four input-service executables pass on iPad then iPhone with one booted at a time. The original engine passes its Caps Lock query and reaches LoadIconA after 17,463,303 x86 instructions with full stack/image/live-heap/register/defined-flag agreement. Initial lock states are explicit software-profile inputs, not sampled Apple hardware. Apple event/message delivery, AttachThreadInput, process teardown, acquisition, visible menus and matches remain unfinished. The next call needs the game's actual icon resource; inspection found group 107 and four icon payloads in the original PE.


LoadIconA now reads integer group-icon resources from an immutable mapped-PE snapshot, checks directory/payload bounds, selects the highest-color 32x32 image and caches an owned shared handle. DIB decoding handles 1/4/8/24/32-bit uncompressed pixels, bottom-up rows, legacy AND/XOR colors and explicit 32-bit alpha mode. Missing resources and malformed inputs return errors; named/system icons, compressed/PNG payloads and scaling remain strict unsupported gates. Tests cover immutable snapshots, cross-process/missing handles, malformed RVA/directory/palette/data guards and 80,000 concurrent cache reads. All 22 native groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer checks, runtime objects compile for all Apple targets, and all five service executables pass on iPhone then iPad with one booted at a time. Original startup selects group 107/resource 2; a native pixel trace matches an independent original-resource decode byte for byte, with payload/pixel/mask hashes recorded. Full differential passes at LoadCursorA(NULL,32512), the standard arrow, after 17,463,307 x86 instructions. Apple icon drawing, visible menus and matches remain unfinished.


The entire original-address engine has now also been compiled from the pinned LLVM source for arm64-apple-ios16-simulator, using simulator-native SoftFloat and Bochs math objects. Original CRT startup runs through the same LoadCursorA checkpoint on iPad and then iPhone, one booted at a time. Separate per-device reports preserve the binary/source identities and full register/defined-flag/stack/image/live-heap/icon comparisons; both pass after 17,463,307 reference instructions. The simulator object is 43,368,448 bytes, SHA-256 f7f001b9e551414d1a7576464302b21d7d1d303460aa408547a52bc32653275e. These are native CLI startup diagnostics inside each simulator, not UIKit touch/visible rendering or an accepted gameplay session. Physical-device execution remains unverified.


LoadCursorA(NULL,IDC_ARROW) now creates an actual shared native resource with immutable 32x32 RGBA/AND-mask pixels and hotspot (0,0), using an explicit original TribesPad software theme. A generator checks theme/table consistency. Native tests cover 80,000 concurrent initial/cache loads, stable handle identity, owning-process separation, fill/outline/hotspot geometry, full transparent/XOR/alpha invariants, missing IDs and guarded lookups. Native startup traces compare every cursor pixel and mask byte to the profile and record hashes. All 23 groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer; runtime objects compile for macOS/device/simulator. All six service executables and the original-engine startup differential pass on iPhone then iPad, one booted at a time. Full register/defined-flag/stack/image/live-heap/icon/cursor agreement holds at GetStockObject(4), BLACK_BRUSH, after 17,463,310 reference instructions. The software theme is not a captured Windows bitmap. Apple pointer installation, SetCursor/ShowCursor, other system shapes, custom cursor resources, shared-resource teardown, visible menus and gameplay remain unfinished.


GetStockObject now retains native stock brushes 0..5 (white/light-gray/gray/dark-gray/black/null) with immutable logical SOLID/NULL style and COLORREF data. Each memory DC starts with white and retains its brush independently from its bitmap; stock brushes can be selected in multiple DCs and survive DeleteObject without losing cache identity. Tests add 80,000 concurrent cache/read/delete cycles, all six styles/colors, guarded and cross-process lookup failures, independent DC selections and stock lifetime checks. All 23 groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer; runtime objects compile for all Apple targets. Seven service executables and original-engine startup pass on iPad then iPhone, one booted at a time. Native startup traces match the requested black and gray descriptors. Both Darkstar Window Class and Curtain register; the latter retains the actual imported DefWindowProcA guest address, so the comparison now admits that specific valid callback alongside original image PCs. Full register/defined-flag/stack/image/live-heap/icon/cursor comparison passes at GetDesktopWindow after 17,463,336 reference instructions. Visible windows, brush drawing/device-specific gray pattern realization, stock fonts/pens/palette/DC_BRUSH, process teardown and gameplay remain unfinished.


Apple display setup now reads NSScreen backing coordinates/current CG display-mode raster on macOS and UIScreen bounds/scale/nativeBounds on iOS, on the main thread, with no synthesized fallback. Startup measured 2940x1912 at scale 2 on macOS, 1206x2622 at scale 3 on iPhone and 2064x2752 at scale 2 on iPad. These are current simulator/host API observations, not physical-device acceptance or a claim about panel refresh performance. The guest desktop owns a bounded opaque BGRA8 software surface; GetDesktopWindow has a stable process-owned identity, GetDC supports the desktop and NULL-screen alias, GetDeviceCaps returns its dimensions/depth, and ReleaseDC validates the acquiring thread/window/process. Released handles fail, thread exit reclaims remaining contexts, and IsWindow recognizes the protected desktop root. Tests cover 80,000 concurrent DC lifetimes, surface bytes/guards, stale/cross-process/foreign-thread operations and root identity/destruction. All 24 groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer; both core and Apple display objects compile for macOS/device/simulator. Eight service executables and original startup pass on iPhone then iPad with one booted simulator. Full memory/register/defined-flag/resource comparisons pass at GetLogicalDriveStringsA after 17,463,369 reference instructions; all six desktop acquisition/query/release calls complete. The comparison replays measured Apple geometry as external input. The owned surface is not a host-desktop capture or onscreen presentation. General context attributes/drawing, rotation/mode/multiple-display updates, shared surface teardown and playable rendering remain unfinished.

Configured drive discovery now implements GetLogicalDriveStringsA and GetDriveTypeA from the existing process-owned C: namespace. Enumeration returns a five-byte MULTI_SZ with correct size-query, short-buffer and success lengths; C: is a declared fixed virtual volume, and NULL selects the current directory root. Absent or malformed roots return no-root-directory; this does not classify unrelated host volumes or invent removable/CD media. Tests cover unchanged short buffers, guarded exact/oversized writes, invalid pointers, missing profiles, case-sensitive separators, lowercase drive letters and 8,000 concurrent process-owned enumeration/type queries. All 24 native groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer; runtime objects compile for macOS/device/simulator. Nine service executables, including the real filesystem fixture, pass on iPad then iPhone with one booted simulator at a time. Full engine startup on macOS and both simulators matches original-x86 registers, defined flags, stack, image and live heap at GetSystemMetrics after 17,463,834 reference instructions. The two drive enumeration calls return 5 then 4, and the original C:\ classification returns DRIVE_FIXED (3). The next observed request is metric 42 (legacy DBCS capability), caller return 5641476. Menus, onscreen game rendering, live controls and authenticated matches remain unfinished.

GetSystemMetrics now shares primary-screen width/height with the Apple-backed desktop and reports DBCS support absent for the existing single-byte CP1252/US text services. Other metrics remain strict gates; 80,000 concurrent dimension/DBCS query sets verify process geometry and LastError preservation. Native unnamed counting semaphores now use typed registry handles and pthread condition variables, with signed count validation, zero/finite/infinite waits, monotonic relative timeouts, bounded release, guarded previous-count output, shared counts across duplication and last-reference cleanup. Tests include 8,000 concurrent wait/release cycles, finite timeout behavior, stale/wrong-type handles, source-close duplication and maximum-count/previous-output failures. The original semaphore wrapper really passes -1 as its maximum (push at 0x55b0dc); three creations correctly fail with ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER (87), and the original zero-handle poll correctly returns WAIT_FAILED with ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE (6). This preserves the actual error path rather than interpreting -1 as an unlimited count. All 25 groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer; runtime objects compile for macOS/device/simulator. Ten service executables and full original-engine startup pass on iPhone then iPad, one booted simulator at a time. Register/defined-flag/stack/image/live-heap comparisons agree at FindFirstFileA after 17,668,895 reference instructions. Its first request is the real asset pattern base\*, with output at guest 3136116 and caller return 5593585. The downloaded base directory contains 30 native entries, including 21 VL2 archives; actual enumeration is the next implementation boundary. Named/security/interprocess synchronization, other waitable types/multiple-object waits, cancellation, full process inheritance, visible rendering, controls and authenticated gameplay remain unfinished.

FindFirstFileA, FindNextFileA and FindClose now enumerate the actual mounted assets through owned native snapshots and a separate typed handle arena. Each result is a guarded 320-byte x86 WIN32_FIND_DATAA with names, directory/archive/owner-mode-readonly/native-hidden attributes, creation/access/write FILETIME values, 64-bit regular-file sizes and an empty short-name field. ASCII literal/star matching is case-insensitive; *.* includes extensionless entries and a trailing .* may match no extension. Results sort deterministically without claiming Windows directory order. Caller pattern changes cannot alter an active search; exhaustion preserves output and reports error 18, stale/foreign-base handles fail, and close frees owned metadata. Native tests exercise 8,000 concurrent lifetimes, exact fixture timestamps/layout, >4 GiB sparse sizes, guards, literal/wildcard/directory matching and link containment/unsupported-link failures. The case-collision fixture exposed a host/Simulator difference: distinct case variants cannot be created, and Simulator open/stat disagree on alternate-case lookup. The corrected test verifies directory entries instead; rejection of two physically distinct case names remains unverified on this filesystem. Runtime lookup already resolves through actual directory entries. All 26 groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer, and runtime objects compile for macOS/device/simulator. Eleven service executables and full startup pass on iPad then iPhone, one booted at a time. Original startup completes 277 search calls and receives 257 entries, including dot entries, across ten real asset directories; every search closes. Independent host inventory checks names, order, attributes, sizes and stable timestamps before replaying captured metadata to original x86; access times are shared external inputs and Python birth-time checks allow four FILETIME ticks for float precision. Full register/defined-flag/stack/image/live-heap comparisons agree at CreateFileA after 18,153,290 reference instructions on macOS and both simulators. The pending request opens base\audio.vl2 (35,233,672 bytes), GENERIC_READ, share 3, OPEN_EXISTING and flags 0x08000080 (sequential scan/normal), caller return 5592541. DOS question-mark/trailing-period and short-name behavior, full ANSI/Unicode, reparse points, process teardown, actual file reads/archive mounting, rendering, live controls, QoL integration and authenticated gameplay remain unfinished.

CreateFileA now opens existing regular files in the configured guest namespace with real owned descriptors, requested generic read/write rights and normal/sequential-scan flags. File sharing checks both directions under the native handle registry lock and identifies files by device/inode, including hardlinks. Descriptor duplicates retain the original sharing reservation until the last owned handle closes; denied opens close their temporary descriptor. Tests cover real bytes, case-insensitive paths, exclusive/shared/read-vs-write conflicts, hardlink identity, retained reservations after source close, missing-file/path/directory errors and 8,000 concurrent open/read/close lifetimes. Zero-access metadata opens use O_EVTONLY; testing showed Darwin can still read such descriptors, so explicit typed-borrow checks enforce guest content rights instead of relying on that flag. Future ReadFile/WriteFile must use those checks. All 27 native groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer; runtime objects compile for macOS/device/simulator. Twelve service executables and original startup pass on iPhone then iPad with one booted simulator at a time. Full register/defined-flag/stack/image/live-heap comparisons agree at GetFileSize after 18,153,375 reference instructions on all three execution targets. The real base\audio.vl2 opens as handle 0xe1000007, using GENERIC_READ, sharing 3 and flags 0x08000080; independent host inspection records 35,233,672 bytes and SHA-256 88668510265840cc65efda854f0064251cd7d23b2ab125c4d911455ac12f3b5c. The pending size query uses high-word output guest 3136100, caller return 5592864. This proves opening, not native engine consumption or archive mounting. Create/truncate/security/template/overlapped/device modes, unmanaged interprocess share enforcement, pending-I/O cancellation/close, size/read/seek/write services, renderer, live controls, QoL integration and authenticated gameplay remain unfinished.

GetFileSize, SetFilePointer and synchronous regular-file ReadFile now operate on native descriptors. Size queries return guarded low/high DWORDs and clear LastError to disambiguate valid 0xffffffff low values. Seeking handles signed 32/64-bit distances, begin/current/end, guarded high-word in/out, beyond-EOF positions and negative/overflow rejection; duplicate handles share the native cursor. ReadFile enforces guest read rights, validates output/range arithmetic, clears byte count on failure, handles full/partial/EOF/zero-byte reads and retries EINTR. Overlapped/nonregular I/O and write services remain unfinished, and callers must synchronize compound shared-cursor sequences. Tests cover sparse size boundaries through 0x1ffffffff, sentinel success versus failure, output guards, signed/64-bit/negative/overflow/shared-duplicate seeks, metadata-access denial and 8,000 concurrent Win32 open/read/close lifetimes. All 27 groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer; objects compile for all Apple targets. Twelve service executables pass on both simulators, one booted at a time. Full startup comparisons pass on macOS, iPad and iPhone after 108,799,597 original-x86 instructions, including all defined-register/flag/stack/image/live-heap comparisons. The reference instruction budget is now one billion; 30 million and then 100 million ended before the archive/resource lookup stage completed, without implying an engine failure. Startup opens/closes 26 files, performs 144 size queries, 646 seeks and 210 reads, consuming 1,415,804 actual bytes. These include all 21 base VL2 archives, the selected yHDTextures2.0.vl2 index, console_start.cs, compiled default scripts and client/server prefs; this is archive/startup-data consumption, not texture presentation or a controllable map. The next boundary is the decorated IFC22 CImmDevice::CreateDevice import (cdecl), called with image 0x400000 and HWND 0, return 0x50d3e7. Direct binary inspection distinguishes original IFC22 SHA 344543cad6ed6f60cf36b6c3add4f7ec686b2bb7e2ca6f59ab926e7bb6df0adf (factory 0x100066d0, entry 0x10018fcc) from selected QoL SHA eaf5a42d301cc64a6e526aa49a82dbaefe655ed1ac0ba67939d0bab2fb6ab607 (factory 0x10055e10, entry 0x100f9b58). The QoL factory is exactly xor eax,eax; ret (33c0c3), but initialization applies required patches. No stand-in IFC22 provider was added: translating that export without initialization would not prove the product. Loader initialization/validated precompiled patch routing, AVX requirements, rendering, live input and authenticated gameplay remain unfinished.

## Next goal-loop iteration

1. Establish an observable desktop product reference and capture startup failure evidence. Verify patch signatures and installation integrity before relying on the reference. Do not buy or alter the user's existing CrossOver license.
2. Continue the verified original-address AOT runtime through native file open/read/seek and actual archive mounting, then cooperative level, buffered-event delivery, acquisition and polling. Native provider load/export routing and root COM lifetimes are implemented. Feed actual Apple keyboard, pointer, touch and controller state through the required device interfaces and original callbacks, then advance subsequent startup services. Extended80 helpers and arithmetic C1 are implemented; exceptional behavior, full fault delivery and later engine paths remain acceptance gates. Keep unsupported paths as strict failures.
3. Capture IFC22 initialization and code-write behavior, preserve guest addresses, and construct validated precompiled patch targets. The static candidates alone do not close this gate.
4. Reach the actual game's menus and a stock map through a native renderer and runtime. Then bind the tested Apple input sources to the game's real controls and repeat simulator testing sequentially.
5. Preserve normal authentication, validate controlled multiplayer before community matches, load the selected HD assets without silent reduction, and complete physical-device acceptance.

The standalone native shell and UDP probe are intermediate diagnostics. They do not change the selected product requirements.

## References used in this iteration

- [Official TribesNEXT distribution](https://www.tribesnext.com/), [QoL preview notes](https://www.tribesnext.com/forum/discussion/4430/preview-qol-fixes-update).
- [PlayT2 texture catalog](https://playt2.com/textures), with the complete encrypted share link pinned in the manifest.
- [Official live server list](https://www.tribesnext.com/list.json), rediscovered by the app before queries.
- [QStat's primary protocol implementation](https://github.com/rocketsciencegg/qstat/blob/main/qstat.c), pinned as a protocol reference in `dependencies.lock.json`; its runtime code is not embedded in the Apple app.

Architecture reference: [Intel SDM revision 089](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/868137/325462-089-sdm-vol-1-2abcd-3abcd-4.pdf), FTST at PDF page 1089 (Volume 2A, page 3-393). Its downloaded hash is pinned in `dependencies.lock.json`.

Extended precision foundation: [Berkeley SoftFloat 3e](https://www.jhauser.us/arithmetic/SoftFloat.html), [library interface](https://www.jhauser.us/arithmetic/SoftFloat-3/doc/SoftFloat.html). The archive is hash-pinned and its redistribution license is retained under `port/licenses/SoftFloat-3e.txt`.

Win32 contracts used for startup/resource integration: [STARTUPINFOA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/ns-processthreadsapi-startupinfoa), [GetModuleHandleA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/libloaderapi/nf-libloaderapi-getmodulehandlea), [GetModuleFileNameA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/libloaderapi/nf-libloaderapi-getmodulefilenamea), [CreateCompatibleDC](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/wingdi/nf-wingdi-createcompatibledc), [CreateCompatibleBitmap](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/wingdi/nf-wingdi-createcompatiblebitmap), [RegisterClassA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-registerclassa), [UnregisterClassA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-unregisterclassa).

Additional native-runtime contracts: [CreateWindowExA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-createwindowexa), [WSAStartup](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winsock/nf-winsock-wsastartup), [GetCurrentDirectory](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-getcurrentdirectory).

Guest version-info contract: [GetVersionExA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/sysinfoapi/nf-sysinfoapi-getversionexa), [OSVERSIONINFOEXA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winnt/ns-winnt-osversioninfoexa).

Legacy DirectInput ABI references: [Wine interface declarations](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/master/include/dinput.h), [Microsoft device creation contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee416359(v=vs.85)). Native provider implementations here are independent code.

Enumeration layout/behavior references: [Microsoft DIDEVICEINSTANCE](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee416610(v=vs.85)), [Microsoft EnumDevices](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee417804(v=vs.85)).

Device metadata contracts: [Microsoft GetCapabilities](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee417892(v=vs.85)), [Microsoft DIDEVCAPS](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee416607(v=vs.85)).

Object enumeration references: [Microsoft enumeration callback documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee416594(v=vs.85)), and the original [Wine ABI declarations](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/master/include/dinput.h). The implementation and software input catalog here are independent.

Range and data-format references: [Microsoft GetProperty](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee417908(v=vs.85)), [DIPROPRANGE](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee416640(v=vs.85)), and [DIDATAFORMAT](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee416606(v=vs.85)).

Caller-format references: [Microsoft DIOBJECTDATAFORMAT](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee416633(v=vs.85)), [SetDataFormat](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee417925(v=vs.85)).

Buffer references: [Microsoft SetProperty](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee417929(v=vs.85)) and [GetDeviceData](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee417894(v=vs.85)). The latter defines the N-minus-one event capacity and peek/discard semantics.

Keyboard references: [Microsoft MapVirtualKeyA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-mapvirtualkeya), [Microsoft US keyboard layout sample](https://github.com/microsoft/Windows-driver-samples/tree/main/input/layout/kbdus), and [ToAscii](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-toascii). The runtime implementation is independent code using an explicit software profile.

Processed keyboard contracts: [Microsoft GetKeyState](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-getkeystate) and [GetKeyboardState](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-getkeyboardstate).

Icon contracts: [Microsoft LoadIconA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-loadicona), [resource formats](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/menurc/resource-file-formats), and [icon lifetime](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/menurc/using-icons).

Cursor and stock-object contracts: [Microsoft LoadCursorA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-loadcursora), [cursor resource lifetime](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/menurc/about-cursors), and [GetStockObject](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/wingdi/nf-wingdi-getstockobject).

Stock-brush data reference: [Wine GDI object declarations](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/master/dlls/win32u/gdiobj.c). Only simple logical style/color facts were used; the registry, lifetime and selection implementation is independent code.

Desktop contracts: [GetDesktopWindow](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-getdesktopwindow), [GetDeviceCaps](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/wingdi/nf-wingdi-getdevicecaps), and [Apple UIScreen nativeBounds](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/uikit/uiscreen/nativebounds).

Drive discovery contracts: [GetLogicalDriveStringsA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-getlogicaldrivestringsa) and [GetDriveTypeA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-getdrivetypea).

Metric and synchronization contracts: [GetSystemMetrics](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-getsystemmetrics) and [CreateSemaphoreA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-createsemaphorea).

File search contracts: [FindFirstFileA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-findfirstfilea) and [file searching](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/fileio/searching-for-one-or-more-files).

Existing-file opening contract: [CreateFileA](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-createfilea).

File-data contracts: [GetFileSize](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-getfilesize), [SetFilePointer](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-setfilepointer), [ReadFile](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-readfile) and [synchronous EOF behavior](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/desktop/FileIO/testing-for-the-end-of-a-file).

IFC22 initialization diagnostic: `scripts/trace-qol-initialization.py` maps the pinned original engine and selected QoL DLL, initializes its 21-byte TLS template and index, and executes its TLS callback before DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH. The callback returns after 25 instructions. Four explicitly modeled security-cookie inputs sample host UTC FILETIME, performance counter, process ID and thread ID; an independent calculation verifies the executed cookie and complement. An explicit SSE2 feature query explores the current no-SSE2 profile (247 total instructions) and a separate diagnostic SSE2 profile (359). Both stop at LoadLibraryExW for `api-ms-win-core-synch-l1-2-0`, flags 0x800, before any executable-code writes. Unknown services and undeclared sparse TEB reads stop; dependencies have not been initialized. This is original-x86 execution in Unicorn under a declared loader contract, not ARM64 module execution or a real Windows loader. Native CPU capabilities remain unchanged, and native QoL initialization, SIMD translation and validated precompiled patch routing remain unfinished. Reports: `generated/qol-initialization/no-sse2.json` and `generated/qol-initialization/sse2.json`. API contracts: [UTC time](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/sysinfoapi/nf-sysinfoapi-getsystemtimeasfiletime), [counter](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/profileapi/nf-profileapi-queryperformancecounter), [process ID](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-getcurrentprocessid), [thread ID](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-getcurrentthreadid), [processor features](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-isprocessorfeaturepresent).

Native LoadLibraryExW now follows the existing legacy XP API profile: SEARCH_SYSTEM32 (0x800) fails with error 87, and flags zero converts guarded ASCII UTF-16 names to the registered native-provider namespace. Other flag modes, Unicode beyond ASCII and unavailable providers stop explicitly. Tests cover unaligned wide input, case/extension/full paths, invalid arithmetic/hFile, empty/unterminated names, reference counts/export lookup and 64,000 concurrent ANSI/wide load/release cycles. All 27 host groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer; all three Apple targets compile. Thirteen service executables now pass on iPad and iPhone simulators, sequentially with one booted. The loader trace bridges the actual C flag-rejection/GetLastError helpers and reaches unflagged kernel32 loading after 403 original instructions (no-SSE2) or 515 (diagnostic SSE2), with verified cookie/complement and no executable patches yet. Both reports were refreshed. Kernel32 provider publication and later initialization remain unfinished; the earlier whole-engine bootstrap result predates these loader changes and has not been rerun in this checkpoint. [LoadLibraryExW contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/libloaderapi/nf-libloaderapi-loadlibraryexw).

InitializeCriticalSectionAndSpinCount now initializes owned native recursive mutexes and performs the configured try-lock iterations before blocking; single-processor hosts use zero spin. The BOOL helper preserves LastError on success and reports allocation/resource exhaustion as error 8. Tests exercise spin 4000, recursion, contended try/wait, 160,000 protected updates, guard bytes and independent/lifetime-reused objects. All 27 host groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer on current sources, all Apple targets compile, and fourteen service executables pass on both simulators with one booted at a time. The QoL diagnostic now uses actual native loader reference/export lookup, critical-section and TLS helpers through a nonexecuting host mirror. Its limited kernel32 provider defines four TLS exports; only explicitly declared XP-absent exports may return absent, while unknown exports stop. The original initializer observes InitializeCriticalSectionEx/FlsAlloc/FlsSetValue absence and executes its spin-count/TlsAlloc/TlsSetValue fallbacks. It reaches VirtualProtect(address 0x10475000, size 128, PAGE_READONLY 2, old-protection output 0x2ffe30) after 842 instructions (no-SSE2) or 954 (diagnostic SSE2), with independently verified security cookie/complement and no executable code writes yet. This is not ARM64 QoL initialization or provider publication in the engine. The next boundary is real page protection, followed by initialization-write tracing and validated precompiled patch routing. [Spin initialization](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/synchapi/nf-synchapi-initializecriticalsectionandspincount), [Vista-only extended initialization](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/synchapi/nf-synchapi-initializecriticalsectionex), [FLS minimum OS](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fibersapi/nf-fibersapi-flsalloc).

The QoL trace now applies original PE header/section permissions and enforces VirtualProtect through Unicorn 4 KiB pages. Protecting the 128-byte .fptable region changes PAGE_READWRITE 4 to PAGE_READONLY 2 and writes the old value to the guarded stack output. Four diagnostic tests execute actual x86 accesses to verify read-only/no-access/non-executable faults, restoration, adjacent/straddling pages, first-page old protection, and rejection without mutation for distinct reservations, malformed ranges and invalid outputs. Unsupported protection modes stop instead of inventing error results. Code-write attempts now record post-instruction bytes and completion separately; faulting writes cannot count as completed patches. This is not native Apple page enforcement: the host page size is 16 KiB, so the native port still needs faithful 4 KiB software checks or another validated strategy.

The selected QoL module also directly imports InitializeCriticalSectionEx, despite its CRT having a legacy fallback. The flags-zero native helper now shares owned recursive/spin-count synchronization with BOOL return, and the diagnostic kernel32 provider publishes it alongside four TLS exports. The earlier diagnostic GetProcAddress absence for this API is superseded; only explicitly declared FLS APIs remain absent in that profile. Native GetVersionEx stays a compatibility identity, not an assertion that every later Windows API is absent. Current sources pass all 27 host groups normal/ThreadSanitizer, compile on all Apple targets, and pass fourteen service executables on both simulators sequentially. Native Ex helpers initialize sixteen sections in the QoL trace. Both profiles now reach GetProcessHeap (1157 original instructions without SSE2 feature query, 1269 with the diagnostic SSE2 query), with the cookie/complement verified and no executable-code write attempts. CPUID/timestamp/XGETBV observations are recorded separately because Unicorn CPU behavior does not prove the native CPU profile. Native QoL startup, heap services, 4 KiB permissions, SIMD translation, patch routing, rendering, controls and authenticated matches remain unfinished. [VirtualProtect](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/memoryapi/nf-memoryapi-virtualprotect), [extended critical sections](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/synchapi/nf-synchapi-initializecriticalsectionex).

Native GetProcessHeap/HeapAlloc/HeapFree now use a stable per-window owned handle and a distinct 256 MiB arena at 0x50000000..0x60000000. Blocks pack at x86 8-byte alignment, commit native pages as needed, preserve neighboring allocations, and coalesce freed ranges without decommitting shared pages. HeapAlloc supports flags 0/HEAP_ZERO_MEMORY 8 and preserves LastError on failure as documented; zero initial contents are the deterministic allocation policy. HeapFree supports flags zero and checks managed ownership. Tests include 4096 live one-byte blocks in 32 KiB, reuse/neighbor integrity, zero-byte allocation, overflow, stale/interior/cross-window ownership and 8000 concurrent lifetimes. All 28 host groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer, all Apple targets compile, and fifteen service-driver executables pass on both simulators sequentially. An additional executable executes actual emitted ARM64 heap wrappers on macOS (normal/TSAN), iPad and iPhone, checking 1000 allocation/free calls, unaligned stdcall frames, argument/stack guards, full CPU preservation and OOM returns. This is service ABI verification, not gameplay.

The QoL trace executes native GetProcessHeap and reaches unflagged LoadLibraryExW for kernelbase after 1403 original instructions (no-SSE2) or 1515 (diagnostic SSE2). HeapAlloc/HeapFree have not yet been called by this initializer; their allocation semantics are verified separately. No executable patch writes have been observed. HeapCreate/private heaps, realloc/size/validation/info APIs, exception/no-serialization modes, window-teardown metadata release, native 4 KiB enforcement and ARM64 QoL integration remain unfinished. The whole-engine bootstrap link source list includes the new process-heap implementation and parses successfully; its full differential has not been rerun at this checkpoint. [Default heap](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/heapapi/nf-heapapi-getprocessheap), [allocation contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/heapapi/nf-heapapi-heapalloc).

The initialization diagnostic now registers separate kernel32/kernelbase native-provider metadata with shared implemented exports and independent handles/load references. New core regression coverage verifies forwarded export tokens, independent references and lookup failure after release; all 28 host groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer and all fifteen service-driver executables pass on both simulators sequentially.
