Runtime core sources were unchanged in this checkpoint. The trace bridges actual Enter/Leave/Try/DeleteCriticalSection and SetLastError helpers for known initialized objects. Its optional FlsGetValue2 lookup uses an explicitly absent diagnostic export; other unknown names still stop. The original function-pointer cache goes through enforced read-only/writable/read-only transitions and is synchronized by a real native section.

Both trace profiles now stop at a direct FlsAlloc import (1492 instructions without the SSE2 query; 1604 with the diagnostic query), requesting callback 0x10113eff. Its 33-byte entry is pinned by SHA fa0ed7e3b3ed206617d6f8e95b96b876079a6e74c3771c8202ff458ddbf75d77; disassembly shows the NULL check, cleanup calls and stdcall ret 4. Native FLS ownership and real callback/lifecycle dispatch are the next requirement; the callback cannot be discarded or replaced with TLS storage. Heap allocations still have not been reached in this initializer, and no executable code writes were observed. Module metadata here is a diagnostic provider contract, not full Windows DLL loading or ARM64 QoL integration. Native 4 KiB enforcement, FLS callbacks, SIMD translation, patch routing, rendering, controls and authenticated matches remain unfinished.

Owned FLS storage and cleanup are now implemented in `native_fls.c`, with a separate CPU adapter in `guest_fls.c`. The registry isolates process windows and storage fibers, reserves 128 indices, clears values on release and invokes cleanup outside its mutex. Index reuse waits for its captured callbacks. Tests cover index release, explicit storage-fiber deletion, native pthread exit, reentrancy, two-fiber isolation, live-thread reuse, independent windows and 8000 concurrent set/get cycles. The ARM64 adapter owns a 64 KiB guest cleanup stack per bound thread/window, so thread-exit callbacks do not retain a temporary CPU pointer. Actual emitted stdcall wrappers and finite guest callback fixtures verify cleanup, guest allocation disposal, reentrant allocation/free, register/flag preservation and stack guards on macOS (normal/TSAN), iPad and iPhone. All 29 host groups pass normal/TSAN; updated services compile for all three Apple targets. Sixteen service-driver executables now pass on both simulators, one booted at a time, plus separate heap/FLS ABI tests. This is storage lifecycle support, not a Win32 fiber execution scheduler; cross-thread fiber migration, callback repopulation during teardown and process-window teardown remain unfinished. Nonzero repopulation during a draining context stops explicitly.

The current QoL diagnostic exposes real native FLS allocation/get/set services and retains both original cleanup callback addresses. It stops before FLS free because the x86 diagnostic callback bridge is not implemented; the independently tested ARM64 callback adapter is not claimed to execute the QoL module. Startup now reaches GetStartupInfoW after 3650 instructions without the SSE2 query, or 3762 with it. It allocates 868-byte and 3584-byte blocks through the actual native process heap, stores the CRT thread-data pointer in FLS, and initializes the owned native critical-section array. No executable code writes have been observed. These reports supersede the preceding FlsAlloc boundary; the next service is guarded wide startup metadata. Native 4 KiB protection, SIMD translation, executable patch routing, native QoL startup, rendering, live controls and authenticated gameplay remain open.

Wide launch APIs now execute through generated native ARM64 wrappers. GetStartupInfoW shares the original 68-byte layout with GetStartupInfoA because the configured string fields are NULL. A new explicit UTF-16LE command-line configuration retains code units in process-owned storage, separate from the existing ANSI buffer. Returned addresses remain stable and configuration changes after first use are rejected. The host supplies matching representations; general Unicode/code-page conversion, automatic macOS command-line mapping and process/window cleanup are not implemented. [Wide command-line lifetime](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processenv/nf-processenv-getcommandlinew).

Current host service checks pass all 29 groups in normal and ThreadSanitizer modes. Environment tests additionally cover unaligned wide configuration, non-ASCII/surrogate retention, interior NUL/odd-length rejection and 8000 concurrent wide reads alongside environment snapshots. The new launch ABI executable passes on macOS (normal/TSAN), iPhone and iPad sequentially: 1000 guarded unaligned startup outputs and 2000 actual emitted ANSI/wide command-line calls preserve LastError, argument/stack guards and complete CPU state except documented returns. All three Apple target service builds match current source/binding hashes. These CLI tests do not establish Apple UI or native QoL integration.

The QoL original-x86 diagnostic now bridges GetStartupInfoW, GetStdHandle, GetFileType, GetCommandLineA and GetCommandLineW to their actual native services. It captures real initial native handles and explicitly configures matching ASCII/UTF-16LE launch text. Service argument counts are checked against native binding contracts before returning. Both trace profiles stop at GetACP: 4036 instructions without the SSE2 query, 4148 with it, with no executable code writes observed. This supersedes the previous GetStartupInfoW boundary. The next requirement is a coherent ANSI/OEM encoding profile and the CRT's actual conversion dependencies; unknown encoding services still stop. Native QoL initialization, patch routing, SIMD, rendering, live controls and authenticated multiplayer remain unfinished.

Encoding metadata now uses an explicit English legacy profile: ANSI 1252 and OEM 437, consistent with the existing single-byte desktop contract and configured launch text. GetACP/GetOEMCP preserve LastError; IsValidCodePage recognizes the two implemented pages and stops on unknown queries. GetCPInfo supports their explicit IDs plus CP_ACP/CP_OEMCP selectors, writes the original 20-byte x86 SBCS layout with maximum character size 1, question-mark default and zero lead-byte ranges, and checks NULL/range overflow. Unsupported pages stop rather than returning an invented invalid-page result. [Code-page metadata](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winnls/ns-winnls-cpinfo), [installed-page validity](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winnls/nf-winnls-isvalidcodepage).

All 30 host groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer, including 8000 concurrent guarded encoding-metadata reads. The new encoding ABI executable passes on macOS (normal/TSAN), iPhone then iPad, with only one simulator booted: actual emitted wrappers execute 1000 unaligned guarded CPINFO outputs and known-page validity calls, checking complete CPU state, LastError and argument/stack guards. All three Apple target builds match current encoding source and binding hashes. This does not implement encoding conversion or native QoL module initialization.

The original-x86 QoL diagnostic now executes the native metadata services and reaches MultiByteToWideChar(1252, MB_PRECOMPOSED 1, input, 256, NULL, 0) after 5878 instructions without the SSE2 query or 6041 with it. Its captured input is bytes 0..255 with the initial NUL replaced by space; SHA-256 db5552a0efac37c345d5873b247619a4cc27fdaf0c9564480e09e75f4fbd4f2d. No executable code writes have been observed. This supersedes the GetACP boundary; the next requirement is actual size-query and UTF-16 conversion behavior, followed by the CRT's subsequent classification/case dependencies.

Downloaded and pinned two primary Microsoft CP1252 references in `config/encoding-sources.json`. The older [standard table](https://www.unicode.org/Public/MAPPINGS/VENDORS/MICSFT/WINDOWS/CP1252.TXT) SHA f607ae328b4dff5e9bfef725f5fff0ae23f38797f8a5b95998a0d2735c0e8fad leaves five entries undefined. The [WindowsBestFit table](https://www.unicode.org/Public/MAPPINGS/VENDORS/MICSFT/WindowsBestFit/bestfit1252.txt) SHA 72ea23c939c5b26fae7aded0207b327e2f3902d7d3c168d7087f5cfc38ee76a9 agrees with all 251 defined standard entries and explicitly maps 81/8D/8F/90/9D to matching Unicode C1 controls. Acquisition and cross-table validation are complete; the converter is not implemented yet. These data references do not constitute execution against Windows DLLs. Native QoL startup, SIMD/patch routing, rendering, live controls and authenticated multiplayer remain unfinished.

Native MultiByteToWideChar now decodes CP1252 using the generated, SHA-pinned Microsoft WindowsBestFit MBTABLE. CP_ACP resolves to 1252. Flags 0/MB_PRECOMPOSED 1 and their MB_ERR_INVALID_CHARS 8 combinations are implemented; all 256 bytes have explicit mappings in this table. Positive lengths retain embedded NULs; -1 scans through the first terminator and includes it. Size queries report UTF-16 code units and ignore the output buffer. Actual output uses unaligned-safe two-byte writes, preserves LastError on success, and returns documented invalid-parameter/invalid-flags/insufficient-buffer errors for implemented failure modes before writing. Other encodings, composite/glyph modes and partial overlapping buffers stop explicitly; faithful native 4 KiB page checks remain unfinished. [Conversion contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/stringapiset/nf-stringapiset-multibytetowidechar).

All 30 native groups pass normal and ThreadSanitizer checks, with 8000 concurrent conversions added to the encoding test. The emitted ARM64 ABI executable passes on macOS (normal/TSAN), iPad then iPhone sequentially, with 2000 six-argument size/conversion calls plus existing metadata checks. It verifies CPU/flags, stack arguments, unaligned output and neighboring guards, embedded NULs, C1/euro mappings and preservation of short output buffers. All three Apple target builds match current converter source. An independent source-data test parses the primary references separately from the generator, checks every native UTF-16 output for all 256 bytes in all four supported flag modes and both code-page selectors, and agrees with all 251 defined entries of the older Microsoft standard table. No Windows DLL execution is claimed.

The original-x86 QoL initializer now completes its 256-unit size query and conversion through the actual C helper. The independently parsed table also matches every byte of its actual converted CRT table. Both trace profiles stop at GetStringTypeW(CT_CTYPE1 1, input, 256, output): 6400 instructions without the SSE2 query, 6682 with it. The recorded UTF-16 input SHA is 1bbab9ebec48bff318f4a36eb0902d58f8f97d973d771d01090720e7027aefd3. No executable code writes have been observed. This supersedes the preceding MultiByteToWideChar boundary; Windows character-classification and subsequent case-mapping behavior are next. Native QoL module execution, SIMD/patch routing, rendering, live controls and authenticated gameplay remain unfinished.

GetStringTypeW now uses explicit character-type records and a compact index extracted from the published Wine 10.0 NLS file. It supports all 65536 UTF-16 code units at CTYPE1/2/3, retains flags such as C1_DEFINED, and does not infer properties from the Apple locale or Python's Unicode library. This deliberately differs from HaloPad's inferred CP1252 classification generator at several characters. The declared reference is Wine-compatible data, not proof of parity with Windows DLLs or an XP-era NLS version. Negative counts include the first NUL terminator, including negative values other than -1 as specified in Microsoft's contract. Source/output ranges, exact alias and invalid flags are checked before mutation; partial overlaps stop explicitly. Supplementary scalar properties are not combined from surrogate pairs. [API contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/stringapiset/nf-stringapiset-getstringtypew), [Wine 10.0 format reader](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/wine-10.0/dlls/kernelbase/locale.c).

The primary data file SHA is 8f92004f6d245648e98a5b6b206e8fdb3dfe6694251692590f5f9aae1552a943; the tagged reader source SHA is 3c6b3d18709b240845d8c49d7cfdf72591aea7e68b267db5db0508d9d80e4328. `gen-nls-classification.py` validates every lookup path, emitting an 8942-byte index and 147 three-level records. Provenance is recorded in `config/encoding-sources.json`; the Wine repository license notice and reproduction notes are preserved under `port/core/vendor/wine-nls`.

All 30 host groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer, including 8000 concurrent guarded classification calls. An independent parser expands the source file and verifies every one of 196608 code-unit/type pairs against the native helper, with input preservation, unaligned output guards and LastError checks. The emitted ARM64 encoding executable additionally performs 1000 actual four-argument classification calls with complete CPU/stack/argument checks on macOS (normal/TSAN), iPhone and iPad sequentially. All three Apple target builds match current sources. These tests verify the declared data profile, not native Windows behavior or Apple UI acceptance.

QoL startup now executes its CTYPE1 request, and every byte of the actual 256-word CRT classification output agrees with the independently expanded source. Startup reaches GetProcAddress(kernel32, "LCMapStringEx") after 8211 instructions without the SSE2 query or 8493 with it; four real CP1252 size/conversion calls have completed. Unknown export lookup still stops, rather than inventing a NULL fallback to avoid implementing case mapping. No executable code writes have been observed. This supersedes the previous GetStringTypeW boundary. Case-mapping semantics and export routing are next; native QoL initialization, SIMD/patch routing, rendering, live controls and authenticated gameplay remain unfinished.

LCMapStringEx now maps default BMP uppercase/lowercase using compact case tables extracted from the same pinned Wine 10.0 NLS reference. The supported profile accepts the configured default, invariant, en-US and en names, with pure LCMAP_LOWERCASE 0x100 or LCMAP_UPPERCASE 0x200. Positive counts retain embedded NULs; any negative count includes the first terminator. Size queries report code units, exact in-place conversion is supported, and short output buffers are preserved on failure. Locale-specific linguistic casing, version/sort contexts, other transforms/locales, partial overlaps and supplementary surrogate pairs stop before mutation. The data remains a Wine-compatible reference, not verified Windows/XP behavior. [Mapping contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winnls/nf-winnls-lcmapstringex).

`gen-nls-casing.py` validates every BMP path in the source tables. An independent parser verifies both directions for all 65536 individual units (131072 unit/direction pairs); NUL separators prevent unsupported supplementary pairs in this unit-level test. Every output code unit in the actual QoL lowercase table also agrees with the reference. All 30 host groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer, with 8000 concurrent guarded case calls added. Actual emitted ARM64 nine-argument mapping wrappers execute 2000 query/map calls on macOS (normal/TSAN), iPad and iPhone sequentially, preserving registers/flags, stack arguments, LastError, input contents and neighboring guards. Updated services compile for all three Apple targets. No Apple UI or native QoL module acceptance is claimed.

The diagnostic kernel32/kernelbase providers now publish the implemented LCMapStringEx token with the existing native module/export lookup mechanism. Original QoL resolves that token through actual GetProcAddress and executes lowercase size/output calls with a default locale. Startup reaches WideCharToMultiByte(1252, flags 0, input, 256, output, 256, NULL, NULL) after 8473 instructions without the SSE2 query, or 8755 with it. The captured UTF-16 input SHA is 15945e044bdd4fa91fa28dce504bb5f509f659688c065e2a0fc0b7aab756354b. No executable code writes have been observed. This supersedes the LCMapStringEx lookup boundary; actual reverse CP1252 conversion is next. Native QoL initialization, SIMD/patch routing, rendering, live controls and authenticated gameplay remain unfinished.


### CP1252 reverse conversion

WideCharToMultiByte now uses all 698 exact/best-fit entries from the pinned Microsoft WindowsBestFit CP1252 WCTABLE. It supports CP_ACP/1252, flags 0 and WC_NO_BEST_FIT_CHARS, size queries, positive lengths with embedded NULs, -1 terminated input, optional single-byte replacement and unaligned four-byte used-default output. Success retains LastError; short output buffers receive the converted prefix and return ERROR_INSUFFICIENT_BUFFER. Composition modes, other code pages, overlapping buffers/BOOL outputs and surrogate input stop explicitly. This implements the declared BMP profile, without claiming full Windows parity. [Conversion contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/stringapiset/nf-stringapiset-widechartomultibyte).

Independent raw-table expansion checks 507,904 code-unit/profile combinations (all 63,488 non-surrogate BMP units, two page selectors, best-fit/strict flags and default/custom replacement). Eight threads complete 8,000 guarded reverse conversions. Each actual ARM64 wrapper run adds 2,000 eight-argument size/conversion calls, checking guest stack, CPU preservation, source/output guards, replacement, BOOL and short-prefix behavior. macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer and iPhone then iPad simulator runs pass sequentially. All 30 host groups pass normal/ThreadSanitizer; macOS, iOS device and simulator core builds pass.

Original QoL now completes six CP1252 size/decode calls, four LCMapStringEx lower/upper size/map calls and two reverse table writes. Both reverse outputs match the independently parsed Microsoft table. The current boundary is InitializeSListHead after 13,913 instructions, or 14,550 with the diagnostic SSE2 query. No executable code writes have been observed. This supersedes the WideCharToMultiByte boundary above. The tracer routes only positive-length reverse conversions with null replacement/BOOL pointers; optional-pointer behavior is tested in the native service and emitted ARM64 wrapper. Native QoL initialization, SIMD/patch routing, rendering, live controls and authenticated gameplay remain unfinished.


### x86 SList initialization and wide module lookup

InitializeSListHead now initializes exactly the original eight-byte x86 header (Next DWORD, Depth WORD, Sequence WORD) with eight-byte guest alignment. Its void ARM64 wrapper retains EAX and all other CPU state while consuming the stdcall argument. It requires exclusive initialization; interlocked push/pop/flush and concurrent reinitialization remain unimplemented. The 1,001-call wrapper test checks both eight-only and sixteen-byte aligned heads, dirty initial headers, adjacent memory, LastError, original stack arguments and independent guest image windows. macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer, iPad and iPhone runs pass, with simulators booted sequentially. [SList contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/interlockedapi/nf-interlockedapi-initializeslisthead).

GetModuleHandleW shares the native registry's named/main image lookup without adding a load reference. The supported ASCII namespace uses unaligned-safe UTF-16 reads, case-independent leaf/full paths, omitted extensions and trailing-dot handling. Missing/unloaded modules return zero with error 126; invalid address arithmetic returns zero with error 87; non-ASCII names stop explicitly. Concurrent tests cover 32,000 wide main/name queries and 64,000 wide provider lookups. Releasing the sole load reference makes the provider unavailable despite preceding wide lookups. [Module lookup contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/libloaderapi/nf-libloaderapi-getmodulehandlew).

The diagnostic kernel32/kernelbase providers now publish GetSystemTimePreciseAsFileTime through the existing native clock_gettime(CLOCK_REALTIME) FILETIME implementation. Actual ARM64 lookup/clock tests perform 1,000 main lookup, wide load, wide provider lookup, export resolution, clock call and release cycles per run. The UTC output falls between independent native samples, with source/neighbor, CPU/EAX/flags, guest stack and LastError checks. This verifies clock format and native sampling; submicrosecond synchronization accuracy has not been measured. macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer and sequential iPhone/iPad runs pass; all 30 host groups and all three Apple target core builds pass. [Clock API](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/sysinfoapi/nf-sysinfoapi-getsystemtimepreciseasfiletime).

Original QoL executes the SList initialization, gets the loaded kernel32 handle via GetModuleHandleW and resolves the real native clock export. The clock export is resolved but has not yet been called by this original startup path. Startup now stops at GetProcAddress(kernel32, "GetTempPath2W") after 13,967 instructions, or 14,604 with the diagnostic SSE2 query. Unknown export lookup continues to stop explicitly. This supersedes the SList boundary above; no executable code writes have been observed. Native QoL ARM64 execution, patch routing, rendering, live controls and authenticated gameplay remain unfinished. The engine bootstrap build includes the new SList source for future linking; the earlier whole-engine bootstrap runtime baseline was not rerun.


### Temporary paths and native startup clocks

GetTempPathW/GetTempPath2W now share the native environment's declared ordinary non-SYSTEM process profile. They select the first nonempty TMP, TEMP, USERPROFILE value, or the explicitly configured Windows directory, using ASCII case-independent variable names. The supported path namespace is canonical absolute ASCII drive paths within MAX_PATH. They return UTF-16 with a trailing backslash and NUL, and return the required size including NUL when the buffer is short or zero. This implementation retains unspecified short-buffer contents and LastError. Sufficient NULL/range-invalid output returns zero/error 87. Non-ASCII/OEM path values, relative/UNC/device paths and normalization modes stop explicitly; SYSTEM identity/impersonation is not supported. The API does not check existence or access rights. [Temporary-path contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-gettemppath2w).

The new host tests cover precedence, empty/case handling, Windows fallback, 8,000 concurrent guarded queries and the output's resolution to the actual configured host temp mount. macOS resolves /tmp through /private/tmp; the integration test checks the canonical host path. The ARM64 wrapper test executes 3,000 classic/2 size, short and exact-capacity calls per run, including odd output addresses, UTF-16 bytes, retained input stack/CPU/flags, LastError and neighbor guards. It also executes 1,000 QPC/frequency pairs against independent native clock bounds and a stable 1 GHz frequency. macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer, iPad then iPhone simulator runs pass sequentially. All 30 host groups and all three Apple target builds pass. The earlier full-engine runtime baseline was not rerun; its launch configuration now supplies the temporary-directory profile for future integration.

The original-x86 QoL trace resolves GetTempPath2W through the native provider, then executes QueryPerformanceCounter and QueryPerformanceFrequency through the existing C clock helpers. Its security-cookie FILETIME/QPC inputs now use those same native helpers, with cookie verification retained; process/thread identity inputs remain host diagnostic samples. No call to the newly resolved temporary-path function has yet been observed in this startup path. Startup reaches GetEnvironmentVariableA("mimalloc_show_errors", buffer, 65), after 18,380 instructions in the latest no-SSE2 snapshot and 18,969 in the diagnostic SSE2 snapshot. Native time/cookie-dependent branch counts can vary between runs. Unknown environment lookup still stops explicitly. No executable code writes have been observed. This supersedes the GetTempPath2W lookup boundary. Native QoL ARM64 initialization, rendering, live controls and authenticated multiplayer remain unfinished.


### Configured environment-variable lookup

GetEnvironmentVariableA now reads ASCII names/values from the explicit per-image guest environment, with case-independent names. It never inherits or exposes the Apple host process's environment. Present variables return their length excluding NUL; size/short queries return the required capacity including NUL. Empty present values return zero after writing NUL (or one for a size query) while retaining LastError. Absent/NULL/empty names return zero/error 203. Sufficient NULL/range-invalid output returns zero/error 87. The implementation retains unspecified short-buffer contents. Source/output aliasing is supported by copying the name before mutation. Non-ASCII/OEM-to-ANSI conversion and oversized names/values stop explicitly; native Windows parity for the declared corner-case profile has not been established. [Lookup contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processenv/nf-processenv-getenvironmentvariablea).

Eight threads complete 32,000 guarded size/short/exact/missing calls across independent guest environments. Core tests cover empty values, NULL/empty names and input/output aliasing. Each actual ARM64 wrapper run executes 5,000 three-argument size/short/exact/missing/empty calls, checking CPU/flags, stdcall stack arguments, source/output guards and LastError. macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer and iPhone then iPad simulator runs pass sequentially. All 30 host groups and all three Apple target core builds pass. The original whole-engine runtime baseline was not rerun.

Original QoL now executes all 36 requested mimalloc option lookups, including mimalloc_show_errors, verbose, stats, eager_commit, purge_delay and retry_on_oom. None are present in the configured TMP/TEMP guest block, so actual native lookup returns zero/error 203 and the original allocator follows its defaults. This is a lookup result against recorded data, not a forced success or a bypass of option initialization. Startup now reaches GetSystemInfo. The latest no-SSE2 snapshot contains 31,849 instructions; the diagnostic SSE2 snapshot contains 32,318. Counts may vary with native clock/cookie-dependent branches. No executable code writes have been observed. This supersedes the first mimalloc environment lookup boundary. Guest memory geometry is the next contract to resolve; native QoL ARM64 initialization, rendering, live controls and authenticated multiplayer remain unfinished.


### System metadata and allocator provider load

GetSystemInfo now writes exactly the 36-byte original-x86 SYSTEM_INFO layout, with four-byte pointer/mask fields. The declared logical guest profile uses 4 KiB pages, 64 KiB allocation granularity and a 0x10000–0x7ffeffff application range. Architecture/type/level/revision match the existing compatibility CPU (x86, Pentium type, family 6/model 3/stepping 3); these fields do not advertise additional instruction support. The processor count reflects native online processors capped at one 32-bit group, with a matching active mask. Its single/multiprocessor distinction agrees with native critical-section spin initialization. [Field contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/sysinfoapi/ns-sysinfoapi-system_info).

Apple backing pages on this host are 16 KiB. Reporting the logical guest profile is not proof of native 4 KiB commit/protection enforcement. VirtualAlloc/VirtualAlloc2FromApp and native subpage protections remain unimplemented; owned heaps commit native backing pages. GetNativeSystemInfo and processor-group/affinity APIs remain separate unimplemented services. This extends the HaloPad reference's memory geometry while using the native processor-count policy rather than its fixed one-processor value.

The actual ARM64 wrapper test performs 1,000 void stdcall calls per run. It verifies exact x86 layout, odd output addresses, neighboring guards, EAX/CPU/flags/stack/LastError preservation, CPU identity against the actual x86_cpuid helper, and native processor count/mask. macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer and iPad then iPhone simulator runs pass sequentially. All three Apple target core builds match current source. The earlier 30-group host service result remains valid for unchanged service sources; the new system-info service is tested by its separate emitted-wrapper suite. The whole-engine runtime baseline was not rerun; its build now includes guest_system.c.

The original QoL initializer accepts the native C system-info output, then executes LoadLibraryA("kernelbase.dll") through the existing registered provider and actual native load-reference service. It next requests GetProcAddress(kernelbase, "VirtualAlloc2FromApp"). The latest no-SSE2 snapshot contains 31,902 instructions; the diagnostic SSE2 snapshot contains 32,266. Counts may vary with native clock/cookie-dependent branches. No executable code writes have been observed. This supersedes the GetSystemInfo boundary. Virtual allocation/lifetime/extended address requirements are the next implementation work; native QoL ARM64 initialization, rendering, live controls and authenticated multiplayer remain unfinished. [Allocation contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/memoryapi/nf-memoryapi-virtualalloc2fromapp).

### Owned virtual-memory backend

VirtualAlloc2FromApp and VirtualFree now have emitted original-x86 seven- and three-argument ARM64 wrappers backed by a locked reservation registry outside guest memory. The private 0x60030000–0x7fff0000 arena is disjoint from both existing heaps. Supported current-process NULL/-1 requests reserve, commit, recommit, decommit and release nonexecutable memory with PAGE_NOACCESS/READONLY/READWRITE. New commits are zeroed; recommits retain existing contents and protections; release requires the original base and zero size. Address-requirement extended parameters use the x86 16-byte parameter / 12-byte requirements layout and constrain lowest/highest addresses plus power-of-two alignment. FromApp rejects executable protection. [Allocation contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/memoryapi/nf-memoryapi-virtualalloc2fromapp), [release contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/memoryapi/nf-memoryapi-virtualfree).

This is a partial VM implementation. Native commit/decommit ranges must cover whole Apple backing pages; an independent 4 KiB commit on this 16 KiB host aborts before allocation rather than returning fictitious success. Reservation tail padding remains inaccessible. Remote processes, placeholders, reset, large pages, write-watch, NUMA/other extended modes, protection modifiers and executable memory remain explicit unsupported gates. VirtualAlloc/VirtualProtect native integration, shared image/heap reservation accounting, teardown and general subpage enforcement remain unfinished.

Each normal ABI run exercises 500 owned lifecycle sequences through the actual emitted wrappers, with stack/CPU/flags guards, zero-fill, recommit retention, mixed committed/reserved regions, aligned address requirements, neighbor retention and invalid release checks. Eight native threads complete another 4,000 allocation/release lifetimes. macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer, then iPhone and iPad simulator runs pass. Separate subprocesses confirm writes to decommitted, released and read-only regions fault, and unsupported 4 KiB commits abort with the stated diagnostic. All three Apple target service builds match current sources/bindings. The whole-engine bootstrap was not rerun.

Original QoL now resolves VirtualAlloc2FromApp and frees its kernelbase load reference using actual native module services. It next requests LoadLibraryA("ntdll.dll"), where the diagnostic stops before inventing a provider. Static original code identifies NtAllocateVirtualMemoryEx as the next requested symbol; that lookup and actual allocation arguments have not executed. The tracer publishes the tested allocation export but allocation calls still stop until owned reservations are bridged into Unicorn. No executable writes have been observed. This supersedes the allocation-export lookup boundary above; native QoL ARM64 initialization, HD rendering, live controls and authenticated multiplayer remain unfinished.

### NT allocation wrapper and provider lookup

NtAllocateVirtualMemoryEx now shares the owned nonexecutable reservation backend through a distinct seven-argument original-x86 wrapper. Its address and SIZE_T arguments point to four-byte in/out fields, rather than carrying FromApp's values directly. It rounds reservation starts down to 64 KiB, commit starts to logical 4 KiB, and ends upward to logical pages; backing-page restrictions still apply. Success writes the actual base/size. Implemented failures return NTSTATUS and retain input fields; every return preserves Win32 LastError. NT recommit retains data while applying the requested protection. Executable NT allocations stop explicitly pending guest patch routing, rather than being given FromApp's executable-protection rejection. [Microsoft signature](https://microsoft.github.io/windows-docs-rs/doc/windows/Wdk/Storage/FileSystem/fn.NtAllocateVirtualMemoryEx.html), [NT allocation contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ntallocatevirtualmemory), [Wine 10.0 NT rounding/recommit reference](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/wine-10.0/dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c).

The declared subset maps invalid handles, invalid pointer ranges, invalid parameters, allocation conflicts and memory exhaustion to NTSTATUS. Native Windows error-precedence parity has not been established. Remote processes, executable mappings, aliasing NT output fields, output fields overlapping nonwritable allocations and previously listed unsupported VM modes remain explicit gates; native guest fault/SEH delivery is still incomplete.

The expanded actual ARM64 ABI suite passes macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer and final iPhone then iPad runs sequentially. Each run includes the earlier 500 FromApp/free lifecycle sequences, 500 NT sequences and a nonzero extended-parameter NT call. Checks cover odd in/out pointer addresses, rounded output sizes, non-64-KiB commit offsets that fit native pages, recommit protection/data retention, conflicts, invalid handles/parameters/pointers, retained failure inputs, NTSTATUS, CPU/flags/stack guards and LastError. Eight threads still complete 4,000 independent shared-backend lifetimes. Separate fault processes verify native protections, unsupported subpage commit and the NT executable-allocation gate. All three target service builds match current source/bindings; whole-engine bootstrap remains unrerun.

The diagnostic now defines a separate ntdll provider with only the tested allocation export, distinct from kernel32/kernelbase symbols. ### Sampled processor identity and UMA topology

GetCurrentProcessorNumberEx now writes the exact four-byte x86 PROCESSOR_NUMBER using Apple's public pthread_cpu_number_np sample: group zero, sampled processor byte, reserved zero. It preserves EAX and LastError through its void wrapper. Samples are checked against the guest SYSTEM_INFO count; an unavailable/out-of-group sample stops explicitly. The host may migrate a thread immediately after the sample, so this does not implement affinity. The guest retains one group (at most 32 online processors) and one UMA node. [Current processor contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-getcurrentprocessornumberex).

GetNumaProcessorNodeEx and GetNumaProcessorNode map valid processors to node zero, and invalid processors return FALSE with the documented 0xffff/0xff node sentinel and provider error 87. GetNumaNodeProcessorMaskEx returns the group's SYSTEM_INFO mask in the exact twelve-byte x86 GROUP_AFFINITY layout, with zero group/reserved words. The UCHAR/USHORT value arguments truncate to their declared widths. Invalid node or pointer-range requests fail with error 87; valid outputs retain LastError. This is a declared single-node guest profile, not discovery of arbitrary host NUMA topology or established native Windows error-precedence parity. Other groups, affinity control and topology beyond the capped guest group remain unfinished. [Node contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-getnumaprocessornodeex), [mask contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/systemtopologyapi/nf-systemtopologyapi-getnumanodeprocessormaskex).

### Wide module filenames

GetModuleFileNameW now exposes loaded module registry paths as UTF-16 through an actual three-argument original-x86 ARM64 wrapper. ### Native SRW ownership and identified allocation exception

The seven SRW services now have actual ARM64 one-argument wrappers: initialize, acquire/release shared or exclusive, and both try operations. The guest object is exactly four bytes and logically opaque. Native mutex/condition metadata tracks each writer, reader and waiting thread outside guest memory. Multiple readers can hold the lock; writers exclude all others. Conflicting try operations genuinely return false. All-zero static initialization is lazy; dynamic initialization writes only four zero bytes. With no holders or waiters, release restores zero and retires native metadata, allowing an idle object to be copied, moved or forgotten without a destroy API. [SRW contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/sync/slim-reader-writer--srw--locks), [initialization/lifetime contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/synchapi/nf-synchapi-initializesrwlock).

Normal services retain LastError; void wrappers retain EAX and CPU/flags/stack.

### Native VirtualQuery reports loader mappings and real provider ownership

`guest_address_space.c` adds a sealed loader mapping profile and the seven-DWORD (28-byte) x86 MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION32 query ABI. Queries round the requested address down to 4 KiB, scan forward through matching allocation identity/state/protection, and retain initial allocation protection when current permissions change. Loader declarations/protection metadata do not themselves change native memory permissions. The original diagnostic derives image/section ranges from the pinned PE headers, declares an explicit image allocation-protection profile of `0x80`, and mirrors actual enforced Unicorn permission changes. This profile is not verified Windows XP/version parity.

Native provider domains are disjoint: GlobalAlloc and process-heap arenas retain permanent logical ownership; their query state comes from actual live GlobalAlloc ranges or the process heap's new committed-page bitmap. GlobalFree removes commitment, while freed packed process-heap blocks retain their native backing pages. Owned VM queries inspect reservation identity, original protection and current commit runs under the actual provider mutex; reserved native tail padding is not advertised as free. Opaque native-provider namespaces are reserved metadata address ranges, not Windows DLL images. Allocation availability outside the existing private VM arena, mapped files, general shared budgeting and teardown remain incomplete.

The emitted `VirtualQuery` wrapper returns exactly 28 bytes, checks declared logical output writability, rejects invalid ranges/buffer lengths and preserves LastError on success. The 24/998 buffer failure codes are the declared ABI profile; Windows-version edge behavior is unverified. Valid queries require a sealed loader profile and otherwise abort explicitly. Existing native full-engine bootstrap does not yet configure this profile and was not rerun.

`scripts/test-virtual-query-abi.py` passes macOS normal and ThreadSanitizer, then iPhone 17 Pro and iPad Pro 13-inch M5 sequentially. Each run checks 800 concurrent real VM reserve/commit/decommit/release lifetimes with 3,200 actual emitted ARM64 queries, forward page ranges, image allocation identity and permission changes, native heap commitment/release semantics, unaligned guarded outputs, error cases and complete CPU/flags/stack preservation. Unconfigured-profile use aborts in a separate subprocess. The affected process-heap host regressions pass normal/ThreadSanitizer with 8,000 concurrent lifetimes; unchanged host groups are retained only after source/test/dependency hash validation. All three native core targets compile with matching current source/binding provenance. Prior broader ABI reports with older hashes remain historical; native 4 KiB enforcement and physical-device execution are not demonstrated.

Both current original-x86 QoL traces pass six actual native queries. The first reports `0x006e0000` as the final 128 KiB of the engine executable section, allocation base `0x00400000`, current protection `0x20`, state MEM_COMMIT and type MEM_IMAGE. The subsequent search observes declared TLS/TEB/GDT ownership and a free 64 KiB range at `0x003b0000`. Startup then requests `VirtualAlloc(0x003b0000, 65536, MEM_RESERVE|MEM_COMMIT, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE)` and stops before allocation. The initializer remains incomplete and engine patch writes remain zero. This executable-memory request requires validated AOT generated-code/patch routing; a guest x86 executable byte buffer cannot substitute for native ARM64 dispatch.

The refreshed zero-TEB control still reproduces the genuine allocator failure with 16 retry sleeps and the same current VM source. Valid identity remains 128 MiB reserved/4 MiB committed with no retries. The native loader/source/binding provenance and updated TEB comparison were checked after all processes completed. Rendering, playable controls and authenticated online play remain unfinished.

Primary layouts and range semantics: [VirtualQuery](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/memoryapi/nf-memoryapi-virtualquery) and [MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION32](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winnt/ns-winnt-memory_basic_information).


### Original generated-code capture produces a guarded partial AOT patch plan

The default diagnostic still stops before low-address executable allocation. Native core sources and their earlier Apple-platform test results are unchanged this turn.

Both captured original QoL startup branches allocate a 64 KiB region at `0x003b0000`, complete 1,391 writes in that region and ten engine executable-section writes, then stop at `InitOnceExecuteOnce`. The write hook now retains before/after bytes and separately records original image-data stores: 1,288 QoL data writes and zero engine data writes are observed in this prefix. Host loader/service-output copies are not represented as original instruction writes. None of the generated instructions execute before this boundary and the initializer remains incomplete.

`scripts/build-qol-capture-plan.py` verifies every observed engine-write preimage against the pinned image, checks the ten modifications are E8 relative-call operands, derives old/new targets and writes an isolated `engine-code-prefix-candidate.exe` for future AOT work. The captured region signature, 64 KiB size and 72-byte x86 trampoline layout agree with [Microsoft Detours v4.0.1](https://github.com/microsoft/Detours/blob/v4.0.1/src/detours.cpp). The pinned source and MIT license are saved with URL/hash provenance in `config/detours-reference.json`; the embedded selected DLL's exact Detours version is unproven. Eleven active trampoline records match their original engine-prefix restore bytes. Their copied prefixes and following E9 branches decode completely, and every branch targets the recorded original continuation. `cbCode` counts moved bytes before the continuation jump, so the plan explicitly includes the following five-byte E9. All ten call changes, eleven trampoline records and the derived engine hash are identical across the two diagnostic CPU branches.

Evidence is in `generated/qol-patch-capture/plan.json`, branch-specific plans and `branch-comparison.json`, plus both explicitly named capture traces. ### Native once initialization and actual QoL callback completion

`guest_once.c` now owns synchronous once state under a native mutex/condition registry, keyed by guest address and window. `InitOnceExecuteOnce` dispatches the actual three-argument stdcall guest callback through `guest_once_callback.c`. A failed callback resets ownership for a waiting caller to retry; successful completion publishes its DWORD context. Both paths preserve callback LastError. Optional context output, DWORD alignment of successful context, and `InitOnceInitialize` reset are covered. The guest object remains four bytes; its compatibility state tags are not a claim about a particular Windows internal representation. Contracts follow [InitOnceExecuteOnce](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/synchapi/nf-synchapi-initonceexecuteonce) and [INIT_ONCE callback](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/synchapi/nc-synchapi-pinit_once_fn).

Each run checks 800 successful calls from eight concurrent threads, three actual failed callbacks/retries followed by exactly one successful publication, shared payload visibility, independent thread LastError, guarded unaligned object/output storage, full caller CPU/register/flags/stack preservation, callback x87 control-word propagation, reset/reinitialization, and NULL output. Callback invocation accounting is thread-local, so another thread cannot weaken the FP-state assertion. Four subprocess cases abort explicitly for recursion, reserved context bits, unowned nonzero word and completion without ownership. Reports include current source/header/binding hashes under `generated/native-win32/once-abi/`. The complete native service library also compiles for macOS, iOS device and simulator targets; the full engine bootstrap was not rerun.

The reference tracer now schedules the original x86 callback on a guarded stdcall frame and resumes its suspended import only after the actual callback returns. It shares the real native once begin/complete state, preserves parent integer registers/flags while allowing thread FP changes, verifies the callback did not overwrite its once word, and reports a pending callback if another unsupported dependency stops execution. The existing native `GetModuleHandleA` helper is connected to this trace, including genuine failed lookup of the absent API-set provider. Both captured CPU branches complete callback `0x10075950` for once object `0x101d6f74`, returning TRUE with context zero. Their next boundary is `GetProcAddress("CompareStringEx")`, and the DLL initializer remains incomplete. No fabricated callback success or absent-export implementation is supplied.

Capture reports and guarded partial patch plans were refreshed. Ten call redirects, eleven trampoline records and the derived engine hash still match across CPU branches; no generated instruction has executed. The refreshed comparison report records report/script/binding provenance. ### Native locale enumeration with reentrant guest callbacks

`EnumSystemLocalesEx` is implemented through `guest_locales.c` and `guest_locale_callback.c`, with an actual four-argument ARM64 stdcall service wrapper and actual three-argument guest callback dispatch. `gen-nls-locales.py` pins and validates Wine 10.0 `locale.nls` and its internal layout header, extracts name-index order and flags, excludes 129 aliases, and emits 958 names: 317 neutral, 626 specific and 15 alternate-sort locales. The data profile is explicit; it is not a measurement of Windows or Apple installed locales. The implementation covers the complete pinned list rather than returning one configured language. No supplemental/replacement locales are present in that source profile. The API follows [EnumSystemLocalesEx](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/win32/api/winnls/nf-winnls-enumsystemlocalesex) and [EnumLocalesProcEx](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winnls/nc-winnls-locale_enumprocex): callback FALSE ends successful enumeration; callback LastError survives; invalid callback/reserved arguments and invalid input flags return errors. Filter combinations follow the pinned Wine reader's union-of-matching-flags behavior.

Each enumeration has its own index and writable guest stack name buffer. Callback changes to that buffer cannot mutate the immutable locale table or another caller. Reentry uses separate scratch space and copied integer register context while preserving shared thread FP state. Callback dispatch and scratch ranges are guarded by configured stack bounds; general guest pointer/exception validation remains unfinished.

Invalid arguments/flags and a stack-bound fault are checked separately. The complete native service library compiles for macOS, iOS device and simulator targets. ### Native UTF-8 conversion and metadata

`guest_encoding.c` now implements CP_UTF8 in the actual six/eight-argument ARM64 `MultiByteToWideChar` and `WideCharToMultiByte` wrappers. Valid Unicode scalar values convert between UTF-8 and UTF-16, including surrogate pairs and legal noncharacters. Strict MB_ERR_INVALID_CHARS/WC_ERR_INVALID_CHARS input fails with error 1113; replacement mode uses U+FFFD and maximal-subpart handling. Overlongs, surrogate encodings, invalid continuation/range bytes, truncation and out-of-range scalars are handled explicitly. Explicit counts preserve embedded NULs, while -1 includes the terminator. UTF-8 flags and default-character/used-default arguments follow the modern [decoding](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/stringapiset/nf-stringapiset-multibytetowidechar) and [encoding](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/stringapiset/nf-stringapiset-widechartomultibyte) contracts, with Unicode scalar/replacement rules checked against [Unicode Chapter 3](https://www.unicode.org/versions/Unicode17.0.0/core-spec/chapter-3/).

Conversion validates and counts before writing; this declared profile leaves output unchanged on strict-input or short-buffer failures rather than claiming Windows-version short-buffer contents. Successful calls preserve LastError. Input/output may be unaligned and must be caller-owned mapped memory. The bounded profile accepts at most 1,048,576 input bytes/code units, and overlapping buffers or unterminated/oversized inputs abort explicitly. General guest memory validation remains unfinished. `IsValidCodePage(65001)` and `GetCPInfo(65001)` now report the implemented codec and its four-byte maximum, with no lead-byte ranges. The default ANSI/OEM profiles remain CP1252/437.

### Native XMM state and legacy SSE moves

The native CPU now has eight IA-32 XMM registers and MXCSR, appended after the existing dispatch mailbox. Existing LLVM prefix offsets remain unchanged (XMM begins at byte 176; MXCSR at byte 304). All ten Python CPU fixture layouts were extended to allocate the full structure. A separate SSE initializer clears the profile's initial XMM state and sets MXCSR to 0x1f80; x87 FNINIT does not reset SSE. Guest callbacks propagate XMM/MXCSR changes through nested callbacks while preserving the established general-register/stack contract. Native SIMD feature advertisement remains disabled until the instruction set is implemented and integrated.

tribes_sse.c now implements legacy MOVSS, MOVLPD, MOVQ, MOVD, MOVUPS, MOVDQU and MOVAPS data movement through finite native helper contracts. MOVSS memory loads clear upper XMM lanes while register copies retain them; MOVLPD loads retain the upper quadword; MOVD/MOVQ clear upper lanes. Register self-aliasing, stores and GPR extraction/insertion retain exact bytes. Aligned memory forms check guest alignment independently of native backing alignment. Invalid register indices, modes, cross-DWORD ranges and invalid register MOVLPD forms explicitly abort. General mapped-pointer validation and guest SEH fault delivery remain unfinished. These helpers are not yet connected to SRW instruction lowering.

The semantics reference is the [Intel instruction-set manual](https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/developer/articles/technical/intel-sdm.html). scripts/test-sse-moves.py executes actual original legacy x86 opcodes in Unicorn to produce 2,112 expected cases, covering all eight registers, register pairs/self-aliases, distinct load/store opcodes, unaligned memory, lane sentinels/random bits, flags and several MXCSR controls. The same corpus passes native ARM64 macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer, iPad Pro 11-inch M5 then iPhone 17 Pro simulators sequentially. Every run checks full CPU preservation outside the expected XMM destination, memory guards, explicit rejected contracts, and repeats the corpus on eight independent native threads. These are native helper tests, not emitted SRW instruction tests or playable sessions.

The actual cdecl/stdcall callback wrapper suite now changes XMM/MXCSR in its nested guest fixture and verifies those changes reach the parent. It passes macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer and both simulators with current header/source provenance. Isolated hidden-window and native DirectInput/root/vtable regressions pass normal/ThreadSanitizer. The old aggregate test inventory had unrelated stale encoding hashes and could not support a fresh selected run; a separate selected-test report avoids treating those historical tests as current. Native core/SSE objects compile for macOS, iOS device and simulator, with CPU/header provenance recorded. Physical-device execution is not claimed.

The full original engine was relinked with the new CPU/header/callback sources and SSE initializer. The first comparison found an initial-state mismatch: Unicorn's reset MXCSR was zero, while native startup used 0x1f80. The reference now explicitly uses the same declared initial state, as it already does for x87. The refreshed macOS differential passes through 108,799,597 original-x86 instructions to the existing IFC22 import stop, including exact final XMM/MXCSR comparison and the previous register/flag/stack/image/live-heap checks. The engine still does not render. Previously completed QoL captures remain original-x86 diagnostic evidence; SIMD move helpers do not establish native QoL initialization.

### Emitted legacy packed SIMD and preserved move/engine baseline

The native SSE helper set and pinned Tribes-specific SRW patch now support XORPS, ORPS, XMM PCMPEQW, PMOVMSKB, PSHUFD and PSHUFLW. Legacy packed computation memory forms use the existing 16-byte guest alignment contract, independent of native backing alignment. ### Independent floating references and native unordered scalar comparison

A new offline x86-64 inline-assembly reference runs the six remaining observed floating SIMD families through Rosetta. It saves/restores host MXCSR, keeps all exceptions masked and records vector output, integer conversion output, arithmetic EFLAGS and MXCSR. The compiler disables the red zone because the oracle uses push/pop for EFLAGS. The deterministic 39,680-case corpus includes signed zeros/infinities, signed QNaN/SNaN payloads, subnormal boundaries, conversion limits/ties, random bit patterns, all four rounding modes, independent DAZ/FTZ settings and preexisting sticky flags. This is a reference comparison, not a physical Intel or native client execution claim.

Comparing identical legacy instruction bytes in Unicorn exposes material differences. Across ADDSD/SUBSD/MULSS/CVTPD2PS, respectively 136/144/179/341 vector results disagree. MXCSR differs in all six families; UCOMISD alone has 888 differences despite identical comparison EFLAGS and unchanged vectors. The current Unicorn reference therefore cannot serve as the sole floating-point oracle. The source, requests, Rosetta outputs, binaries and aggregate report have recorded SHA-256 identities under generated/sse/fp-oracles. Intel's [floating-point reference sheet](https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/developer/articles/technical/floating-point-reference-sheet-for-intel-architecture.html) describes precomputation exception priority and NaN handling; agreement between software references does not replace those architectural rules.

Native UCOMISD register and 64-bit memory helpers now classify/compare binary64 bits with integer operations. Quiet NaNs produce unordered flags without invalid; signaling NaNs add sticky IE. The NaN result suppresses denormal processing. Otherwise DAZ converts subnormal inputs to signed zero, or sticky DE is raised. Signed zero compares equal; signed finite values and infinities use an ordered bit key. OF/SF/AF are cleared and ZF/PF/CF reflect the comparison, while all other flags, XMM values and CPU fields remain unchanged. No host floating-point state is touched. An unmasked IE/DE retains the explicit abort boundary before flag publication; Windows SIMD exception delivery is still unfinished and was not tested by this masked corpus.

All 6,208 UCOMISD reference cases pass the actual ARM64 helpers in two register layouts and unaligned memory, plus equal-bit register self-aliases. Full CPU and memory guards are checked on eight independent native threads. macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer, iPad Pro 11-inch M5 then iPhone 17 Pro simulators pass with matching corpus/current source hashes; only one simulator was booted at a time. The native core compiles for macOS, iOS device and simulator; no physical-device run is claimed.

UCOMISD SRW lowering is still pending, so the previous fourteen-family emitted-instruction total is unchanged. Next work is its strict translator integration and emitted-body tests, then faithful ADDSD/SUBSD/MULSS/CVTPD2PS/CVTTSS2SI behavior, VEX state/decoding and precompiled patch publication. Native full QoL initialization, HD rendering, playable/refined controls and authenticated community matches remain unfinished.


### Strict native UCOMISD lowering and emitted fifteen-family corpus

The pinned Tribes-specific SRW patch now lowers legacy UCOMISD XMM-register and 64-bit memory forms to the verified comparison helpers. Operand roles remain strict, memory widths are corrected independently of XMM register widths, and flag metadata records all six written arithmetic flags. The emitted-body harness now checks post-instruction MXCSR separately from its input. For UCOMISD it executes an independent offline x86-64 Rosetta oracle and records each request/result identity; Unicorn's comparison EFLAGS agree, but its unchanged MXCSR is deliberately not used as the expected state. Exact original bytes and synthetic register/self-alias/SIB/displacement cases pass through strict SRW, LLASM and ahead-of-time ARM64 compilation. ### Strict native truncating single-to-integer conversion

CVTTSS2SI now has native register and 32-bit memory helpers plus strict SRW lowering to any IA-32 destination GPR. Binary32 sign/exponent/fraction bits are handled with unsigned integer shifts; there is no host floating-point call or shared rounding state. Truncation ignores MXCSR rounding control. Signed zero and DAZ-treated subnormal inputs become integer zero. Other discarded nonzero fractions raise sticky precision; integer conversions do not raise denormal exceptions. Exactly -2^31 is valid; larger-magnitude inputs, positive 2^31, infinities and both NaN classes produce masked integer indefinite with sticky invalid. FTZ does not alter integer conversion. An unmasked new exception stops before publishing the destination; Windows #XM/SEH delivery is still unfinished and the masked corpus does not verify it. Intel's [SIMD exception documentation](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/843823/252046-sdm-change-document-1.pdf) identifies integer conversions as exceptions to denormal-operand signaling.

The derived decoder corrects CVTTSS2SI scalar memory to 32 bits and the translator accepts only 32-bit GPR destinations with XMM0–7 or 32-bit memory sources. It emits a typed helper call followed by the destination assignment; EFLAGS are preserved. The complete patch was regenerated against the pinned base, its lock hash updated and the isolated Tribes tool rebuilt. The native core compiles for macOS, iOS device and simulator. No physical-device execution is implied.

The emitted AOT corpus now has 116 observed original sites, 2,644 instruction blocks and 83,096 cases across sixteen SIMD families. Conversion cases include all GPR/XMM combinations and unaligned SIB/displacement memory, 32 exceptional/boundary bit patterns under all sixteen RC/DAZ/FTZ settings, and random inputs/preserved upper lanes. Boundary cases explicitly begin with clear status so preexisting IE/PE cannot hide an error; random cases separately check sticky preservation. Independent Rosetta instruction execution supplies integer/MXCSR expectations, with agreement on Unicorn's integer and EFLAGS outputs; Unicorn's unchanged MXCSR is not used. The reference covers 62,656 floating cases (51,264 CVTTSS2SI plus 11,392 UCOMISD). This remains a software reference, not physical Intel hardware proof.

### Native packed double-to-single conversion and working-precision underflow

CVTPD2PS now has strict SRW lowering and native register/aligned 128-bit memory helpers. Two binary64 source lanes convert to binary32 in the destination's low 64 bits; its upper 64 bits clear. A full source snapshot preserves self-aliases. Conversion uses integer sign/exponent/significand operations, including nearest-even and directed rounding; no host floating state or shared math state is used. NaN signs and retained payload bits survive truncation, signaling NaNs add sticky IE, DAZ makes double subnormals signed zero, and gradual underflow/overflow/precision behavior is reported through MXCSR. Legacy guest memory alignment and unserviced fault boundaries remain explicit.

Intel's [floating-point reference sheet v2.13](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/786447/floating-point-reference-sheet-for-intel-architecture.pdf), page 2, distinguishes the working-precision result with an unbounded exponent from the later bounded/denormal rounding stage. Visual inspection and additional independent x86 reference checks caught an initial overly broad tiny-input rule before this checkpoint: a working-precision rounding carry can reach minimum normal and avoid underflow/FTZ, whereas later bounded rounding can reach the same normal output while still raising UE/PE. Both sides of the signed threshold are now tested. FTZ also flushes exact tiny results with UE/PE. The downloaded primary PDF and rendered flowchart have recorded identities in progress metadata. Unmasked exception delivery and packed-lane exception priority remain unfinished; the tested profile masks floating exceptions.

### Native scalar arithmetic adapter with restored SoftFloat thread state

The new port/core/sse_arithmetic.c adapter computes ADDSD/SUBSD/MULSS candidate result bits and newly raised status using the pinned SoftFloat 3e integer implementation. Guest RC maps to per-thread SoftFloat rounding; working-precision tininess uses the verified after-rounding setting. DAZ replaces subnormal inputs with signed zeros; otherwise their DE is reported. Explicit Intel first-NaN priority avoids the existing 8086 library's different two-NaN payload selection, preserves NaN signs/payloads for subtraction and raises IE for either signaling operand. NaN paths suppress unrelated denormal processing. FTZ flushes exact subnormal results and UE-producing results that later round to minimum normal, with UE/PE. Default invalid results use the pinned Intel negative indefinite NaN.

Every normal SoftFloat entry saves and restores rounding, tininess, exception flags and extended precision. No CPU registers are published by this arithmetic layer; its outputs are candidate bits/new status for the future register wrapper. Guarded outputs, unknown-operation/reserved-control/null-pointer rejection and independent thread context restoration are tested. The existing SSE/engine runtime sources and translator are unchanged by this adapter checkpoint.

### Native VEX scalar double square root

VSQRTSD now has native register/unaligned-memory helpers, a distinct strict decoder mnemonic and emitted calls from unchanged C5/C4 WIG bytes. Only the second source's low binary64 value is consumed; the first source supplies bits 127:64, and the destination's upper YMM half clears after success. This agrees with [Intel's VSQRTSD operand mapping and exception contract](https://www.intel.com/content/dam/develop/external/us/en/documents/319433-024-697869.pdf). Three source/destination roles are snapshotted to handle all aliases. Wrong mandatory prefix, L1 and still-unsupported VSQRTSS forms fail explicitly in the finite flat IA-32 profile.

A separate unary arithmetic adapter uses the pinned integer SoftFloat binary64 square root with all rounding modes and saves/restores all four TLS fields. Signed zeros and positive infinity remain unchanged. NaNs retain sign/payload and become quiet; signaling NaNs raise IE. DAZ converts subnormal inputs to signed zero before invalid classification. Negative nonzero inputs return the negative indefinite NaN and set IE without DE, including negative denormals. Positive denormals set DE; inexact roots set PE. Every positive finite nonzero root is normal, so FTZ/overflow/underflow do not affect it. MXCSR status merges before an unserviced unmasked exception stops; private candidate lanes prevent destination merge or upper-YMM clearing at that boundary. Shared-memory child tests cover invalid, denormal and precision exceptions for both register and memory square roots. Precise Windows fault delivery/priority remains unfinished.

Unused first-source low-lane signaling-NaN/denormal canaries verify that only its upper lane participates. Direct VSQRTSD execution through Rosetta supplies candidate result/status; an independent Python integer-square-root model rounds exact integer radicands and agrees with every new case. Full 440-byte CPU, guarded memory, TLS restoration and eight native threads remain checked. The reference remains software evidence, not physical Intel proof.

### Native VEX ordered single-precision comparison

VCOMISS now has integer-based register/unaligned-memory comparison helpers, a distinct strict decoder mnemonic and emitted calls from unchanged C5/C4 WIG bytes. ### Native VEX scalar single division

VDIVSS now has a distinct strict decoder mnemonic and emits the existing scalar register/unaligned-memory helpers with the new DIV32 arithmetic selector. The integer SoftFloat adapter now dispatches binary32 division alongside multiplication/addition, retaining all four TLS fields and existing NaN/DAZ/FTZ/rounding policies. Masked zero-over-zero and infinity-over-infinity produce the negative indefinite NaN with IE. Finite nonzero division by signed zero produces signed infinity with ZE; infinity over zero produces signed infinity without ZE. Divide-by-zero precomputation suppresses a denormal numerator's DE. NaNs retain first-source payload priority and suppress DE; signaling NaNs set IE. Directed overflow, gradual underflow, working-precision carry, FTZ and inexact status are tested across rounding modes. Intel's [floating-point reference documentation](https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/developer/articles/technical/floating-point-reference-sheet-for-intel-architecture.html) describes the relevant precomputation exception classes; the exact new behavior here is verified by the direct instruction reference. Private candidate lanes and sticky MXCSR merging preserve the full destination when an unserviced unmasked exception stops. Shared-memory child checks cover invalid, denormal, zero-divide, overflow, underflow and precision for register and memory division. These assert candidate-status-before-stop behavior; precise Windows exception delivery and combined-status priority remain unfinished.

Memory accesses validate the guest address range and mapped pointer without requiring aligned guest addresses, consistent with [Intel's VMOVUPS reference](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/782151/253667-sdm-vol-2b.pdf). Aligned VMOVAPS retains its separate alignment checks. Precise guest access-fault delivery remains unfinished.

The combined emitted suite passes 1,486,704 reference cases across 26,906 blocks and seventeen original sites: 1,003,016 register and 483,688 memory cases. Unaligned moves add 1,153 blocks and 49,216 cases across both widths/opcodes, every register alias, C5/C4 WIG and SIB/displacement/ESP/absolute memory. Register-based addresses exercise every legal byte offset in the sixty-four-byte guarded memory region, including accesses ending exactly at its boundary. Cases include zero/all-one/lane-distinct/random vectors, signaling-NaN/subnormal bit patterns, all RC/DAZ/FTZ controls and clear/sticky status. Independent byte-copy/store mapping checks exact width, upper lanes and preserved state. The direct Rosetta oracle now uses an actually unaligned memory operand for VMOVUPS, captures all thirty-two memory bytes, and initializes store memory to the source's complement. Full CPU, memory guards, SoftFloat TLS restoration and eight native threads remain checked.

An initial attempt to use unmasked MXCSR in the Rosetta reference failed before native execution: requested 0x1117 returned 0x1f97. A separate LDMXCSR/STMXCSR-only probe confirmed that this Rosetta environment forces exception masks on (for example 0 becomes 0x1f80). The native implementation was not changed to mimic that behavior. Direct references remain all-masked. Every one of the 49,216 emitted native move cases is separately rerun with all exception masks cleared, retaining its sticky flags and checking unchanged MXCSR plus the independent expected CPU/memory result. The probe and limitation are recorded in the checkpoint. This does not establish precise unmasked arithmetic fault delivery or physical Intel behavior.

### Native VZEROUPPER

VZEROUPPER now has operand-free decoding, no-flags metadata and a strict emitted helper call from unchanged C5/C4 WIG bytes. It consumes no ModRM byte, preserves the following instruction boundary and rejects VZEROALL, reserved vvvv, wrong mandatory selectors, legacy mandatory prefixes/LOCK and the finite profile's non-default address-size form. An unused FS prefix is ignored by the decoder for this operand-free instruction; an initial negative-test assumption was corrected and that case now has positive preservation coverage. No general segmented memory support is implied.

The native helper clears only the eight upper YMM halves in the IA-32 CPU model. It preserves every lower XMM lane, GPR, integer flag, x87/MMX field, MXCSR, pointer field and memory byte. [Intel's VZEROUPPER reference](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/868137/325462-089-sdm-vol-1-2abcd-3abcd-4.pdf) specifies its reserved-vvvv encoding and mode-dependent upper-register clearing. Full-width future ZMM/AVX-512 support is not advertised or implemented.

The combined strict emitted suite passes 1,487,024 cases across 26,911 blocks and eighteen original sites: 1,003,336 register and 483,688 memory cases. VZEROUPPER adds five blocks and 320 cases across its exact original site, both prefix lengths/C4 WIG values and the ignored segment prefix, with zero/all-one/lane-distinct/random vectors, all RC/DAZ/FTZ settings and clear/sticky status. An independent driver model clears all eight upper halves and the native harness compares the entire 440-byte CPU. The direct Rosetta reference captures three canonical YMM registers, verifying their lower-lane retention and upper-lane clearing separately. All 320 new emitted cases are also rerun natively with every exception mask cleared, preserving MXCSR and the other modeled state. The earlier 49,216 unmasked move cases remain included. Rosetta's previously measured forced MXCSR masks remain a recorded reference limitation; precise Windows faults and physical Intel behavior are not proven.

A refreshed independent subtraction model still agrees with all 230,912 subtraction cases. Immutable reports are under generated/sse/vex-zero-checkpoint-20261001. ### Native packed integer moves

VMOVDQA and VMOVDQU now have distinct strict decoder mnemonics and no-flags metadata for both 128/256-bit widths, register copies, loads and stores. Unchanged C5/C4 WIG bytes retain their reserved-vvvv requirements and opcode-6F/7F operand directions. The 66/F3 selectors distinguish aligned/unaligned integer moves; unsupported selectors, extended maps, segment/address overrides and other VEX forms remain explicit. Emitted code reuses the verified aligned/unaligned bit-copy helpers; it does not reinterpret source bits as floating-point arithmetic.

The 128-bit form clears the destination's upper YMM half while copying its lower half; 256-bit forms copy both halves. Stores preserve every vector register and write exactly sixteen/thirty-two bytes. VMOVDQA keeps its guest 16/32-byte alignment checks independently of mapped host alignment, following [Intel's packed integer move reference](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/782151/253667-sdm-vol-2b.pdf). VMOVDQU accepts unaligned guest and host backing. The original absolute-address load at 0x1000c97c keeps its complete bytes and address, with a per-case guest memory base instead of instruction rewriting.

The combined emitted suite passes 1,573,200 reference cases across 29,220 blocks and nineteen original sites: 1,052,488 register and 520,712 memory cases. Packed integer moves add 2,309 blocks and 86,176 cases: both widths/opcodes, all register aliases, three prefix/WIG forms, SIB/displacement/ESP/absolute memory, zero/all-one/lane-distinct/random vectors and floating-point exceptional bit-pattern canaries that remain plain bytes. Unaligned register-address forms cover every valid offset in the sixty-four-byte guarded region. Direct VMOVDQA/VMOVDQU references capture complete vectors and thirty-two memory bytes; store memory starts as the source's complement. VMOVDQU references use actual unaligned backing. An independent byte-copy/store model validates width and upper-lane semantics.

Every one of the 86,176 new emitted cases is additionally rerun natively with all exception masks cleared, preserving MXCSR and the expected CPU/memory state. Earlier unmasked VMOVUPS/VZEROUPPER cases remain included. Four separate emitted load/store probes, one per aligned width/direction, alter a valid guest ESI address by one byte and prove that actual AOT bodies stop before changing any of the full CPU or memory state. Shared-memory parent checks verify the stopped child. Existing helper-level alignment and unmasked arithmetic checks remain. Precise Windows fault delivery and physical Intel behavior remain unproven; Rosetta's measured MXCSR mask limitation is retained in the checkpoint.

### Native VSHUFPS lane shuffling

VSHUFPS now decodes unchanged C5/C4 WIG encodings with four explicit operands: destination, first register source, second register/memory source and imm8. The finite IA-32 profile supports both 128/256-bit widths and keeps unsupported selectors/maps/address modes explicit. The derived udis86 operand array/API, reset behavior and Intel/AT&T printers now support the fourth operand; existing fixup arrays remain three entries because the fourth operand is an eight-bit immediate without relocation. Strict lowering passes the third memory operand's correct fixup/external metadata.

Native helpers snapshot both sources before publishing, including aliased registers. Each 128-bit block selects two 32-bit lanes from each source according to imm8; the 128-bit destination clears its upper YMM half and the 256-bit destination updates both halves. Memory sources accept unaligned sixteen/thirty-two-byte accesses with guest-range and mapped-pointer overflow checks. Every other CPU field, integer flags, MXCSR and memory byte remains preserved. Intel's instruction reference is https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/782151/253667-sdm-vol-2b.pdf. Precise Windows memory-fault delivery is still unfinished.

A new negative test exposed a real decoder bug: generic segment cleanup examined only the first two operands, discarding FS on third-source memory instructions. It now examines all four operands; unsupported FS memory is retained for explicit strict rejection. Regression negatives cover shuffle plus nine earlier VEX families. An initially malformed address-size test was corrected to encode a complete [bx+si] instruction. A separate initial harness classification error treated shuffles as integer moves; native full-CPU comparisons and the independent verifier caught it. The final corpus routes shuffle cases through their own model and reference modes. Neither test error was worked around in the runtime.

The combined strict emitted suite passes 2,605,424 cases across 53,285 blocks and twenty original sites: 1,560,424 register and 1,045,000 memory cases. Shuffles add 24,065 blocks and 1,032,224 cases. All 256 immediate controls are covered for each width and register/memory source, with full register alias coverage for representative controls 00/55/AA/FF/99, three prefix/WIG forms, SIB/displacement/ESP/absolute addresses, every legal unaligned register-address offset and exact guarded-region endpoints. Zero/all-one/lane-distinct/random, signaling-NaN and subnormal bit patterns remain plain lane bytes. Direct references execute actual immediate instructions through a 1,024-entry assembly switch, capturing all vectors and thirty-two memory bytes. An independent integer-lane verifier agrees with every new reference case and all 256 controls in each of its four modes. The refreshed independent subtraction model agrees with all 230,912 subtraction cases. Requests append the immediate field after existing vectors, preserving prior operand-column positions; the native Case ABI remains 696 bytes.

All 1,032,224 new shuffle cases are also rerun natively with exception masks cleared; Rosetta's measured forced-mask behavior remains a reference limitation. Full 440-byte CPU, guarded memory, eight native threads and restored SoftFloat TLS remain checked. Existing unmasked arithmetic/alignment checks and emitted aligned-integer probes remain included. ### Native VEXTRACTF128 half extraction

The finite VEX decoder now accepts the C4 0F3A map's opcode 19 with W0/L1/66 and reserved vvvv for VEXTRACTF128. Its destination is XMM/m128, its source is YMM/256 and its third operand is imm8; the fourth operand remains empty. Host checks cover all 256 immediates in register/store forms, exact original-byte lengths, following RET/reset boundaries, truncated immediate and invalid finite selectors. Strict lowering uses the destination memory operand's fixup/external metadata. Other 0F3A instructions, W1 and unsupported address/memory-segment/extension forms remain explicit finite boundaries; rejecting those forms is not a claim that every such encoding is architecturally invalid.

The native register helper snapshots the chosen source half before publication, including source/destination aliases, copies it into the lower XMM half and clears the destination's upper YMM half. Stores write exactly sixteen bytes to an unaligned guest address and preserve all registers. Only immediate bit zero selects the half; upper immediate bits are ignored. MXCSR, integer flags, other CPU fields and untouched memory remain unchanged. This follows Intel SDM revision 089, local PDF pages 2187–2188 and 2191; source https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/868137/325462-089-sdm-vol-1-2abcd-3abcd-4.pdf. Precise Windows exception delivery remains unfinished.
