Zero/all-one/lane-distinct/random vectors and signaling-NaN/subnormal bit patterns remain pure copied bits. A new 512-entry immediate assembly switch executes direct canonical extraction references; the independent integer-half verifier agrees with all 57,376 new cases and every control value for both destination forms. Refreshed independent shuffle and signed-integer subtraction models agree with all 1,032,224 and 230,912 retained cases. All three proofs bind to the final reference request/result identities. The shuffle verifier now skips other reference modes explicitly; historical proof source is retained in its previous immutable checkpoint.

### Native VEX high/low quadword moves and bounded instruction fixtures

VMOVHLPS/VMOVLHPS now have distinct register-merge mnemonics and strict emitted helpers. VMOVLPS/VMOVHPS distinguish three-operand memory loads from two-operand memory stores. The finite decoder accepts original C5/C4 WIG L0/no-mandatory-prefix bytes, uses vvvv as the merge register for loads/register forms, requires reserved vvvv for stores, and rejects register forms of store opcodes. Memory operands are exactly 64 bits; unsupported length/selectors/maps/address and memory-segment forms fail explicitly. Host decoder checks cover operand roles/widths, each prefix/WIG form, boundaries/reset and rejection cases.

Native helpers snapshot source/merge values before writing aliased destinations. VMOVHLPS places the second source's upper quadword in the lower destination half and retains the first source's upper quadword; VMOVLHPS places the second source's lower quadword in the upper destination half and retains the first source's lower quadword. Loads merge an unaligned eight-byte memory value into the appropriate half. Register/load destinations clear their upper YMM half. Stores copy exactly the selected eight bytes and preserve all registers. Integer flags and MXCSR remain unchanged. This follows Intel's move references in SDM revision 089, https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/868137/325462-089-sdm-vol-1-2abcd-3abcd-4.pdf. The initial compile caught an incorrect new width-constant name; it was replaced with the existing eight-byte TB_SSE_Q access mode before the final successful builds. Precise Windows fault delivery remains unfinished.

The isolated instruction fixture was nearing overlap between synthetic text and actual absolute memory addresses 0x101CB1A0/0x101CB1E0. The combined driver now splits fixtures at 40,000 blocks, verifies every absolute memory operand stays outside each synthetic code span, checks unchanged PE instruction bytes, and reassembles the emitted bodies with global test indices. Current batches have 40,000 and 19,368 blocks. A prefix proof confirms all 54,566 previous emitted LLASM bodies remain byte-identical under batching. This keeps the original absolute operands rather than rewriting them or silently treating original data as fake code.

Zero/all-one/lane-distinct/random vectors and signaling-NaN/subnormal bit patterns remain pure copied bits. Direct canonical x86 references capture full vectors and thirty-two memory bytes; independent integer-quadword selection agrees with every new case and unchanged guard/reference state. Refreshed extraction, shuffle and signed-integer subtraction models agree with all 57,376, 1,032,224 and 230,912 retained cases respectively. All four proof reports bind to current reference identities.

### Addition simulator verification and relocated third-source memory

The packed addition suite now passes on iPad Pro M5 and iPhone 17 Pro, sequentially in that order, matching macOS source/tool/code/corpus/reference identities. Each profile runs all 3,573,712 cases; the macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer and independent 701,952-case packed arithmetic proofs are retained in generated/sse/vex-add-checkpoint-20261001. This completes the pending simulator checks from the previous section.

The initial SRW relocation eligibility check now includes operand two, allowing VEX three-operand instructions with relocated memory sources to reach the existing relocation matching and strict native lowering. An isolated PE fixture retains the original VXORPS bytes c5f85705a0b11c10 and supplies an actual external relocation targeting data at 0x101CB1A0. It reproduces exit 6/-51 before the fix and translates successfully afterward with the same fixture SHA-256. The first fixture attempt omitted the relocations-stripped flag, so SRW did not load its external table; that attempt was not accepted as regression evidence. Preview compiler work ran in a separate cloned tree while the iPhone suite consumed the unchanged main compiler. The reviewed one-clause change was frozen into the main patch only after all addition runs finished.

Actual emitted ARM code passes 65,536 cases on macOS, iPhone and then iPad, with one simulator booted at a time. The relocated symbol maps to synthetic unaligned host data with preserved guards; software XOR expectations verify the full 440-byte CPU, all MXCSR control/status values, aliased input snapshot and upper YMM clearing. The native harness uses runtime integer address comparison for the two linker aliases; its initial direct C comparison was folded incorrectly under separate-object assumptions. ### Native packed square root verified

VSQRTPS now decodes and lowers for C5/C4 WIG 128/256-bit register and unaligned memory sources, with reserved vvvv and explicit rejection of unsupported maps, prefixes, extended roles, segments and address size. New unary binary32 arithmetic uses the pinned integer SoftFloat library and restores all four TLS fields. Input NaNs preserve sign/payload while signaling NaNs raise invalid; DAZ precedes negative-input classification. Positive subnormal inputs raise denormal status when DAZ is off. Positive nonzero roots are normal, so FTZ cannot alter them. Packed helpers snapshot aliases, retain fixed original MXCSR control for every lane, aggregate status and delay full destination publication until exception checks. The 128-bit form clears the upper YMM half. Primary contract: [Intel SQRTPS/VSQRTPS instruction reference](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/782151/253667-sdm-vol-2b.pdf).

An independent exact integer-square-root/rounding model agrees with repeated-lane canonical Rosetta VSQRTPS results/status for 278,848 candidates from 4,357 inputs, all sixteen rounding/DAZ/FTZ combinations and four sticky-status patterns. Inputs include every finite exponent with selected mantissa boundaries, zeros, infinities, signed NaNs, subnormals and deterministic random patterns. The native candidate adapter passes this corpus with masked and mask-cleared controls on macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer, iPhone then iPad. It checks guarded outputs, all TLS fields and eight native threads. Mask-cleared candidate calls do not deliver exceptions; this is the adapter's candidate/status contract. The prior 32,224-case scalar arithmetic adapter regression also passes freshly on macOS normal/ThreadSanitizer.

### Native truncating double-to-integer conversion verified

VCVTTSD2SI now decodes and lowers for original C5/C4 IA-32 WIG L0 F2 register and memory forms, with a 32-bit GPR destination, low binary64 XMM/m64 source and reserved vvvv. W1 is ignored in non-64-bit mode per the [Intel CVTTSD2SI contract](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/774492/325383-sdm-vol-2abcd.pdf), local revision 089 zero-based PDF pages 948–949. L1 remains outside the finite implementation; Intel warns of unpredictable behavior across generations. Unsupported selectors, maps, extended roles, mandatory legacy prefixes, segments and address size fail explicitly.

The new runtime helper uses integer bit classification and shifts, with no host floating-point operations or SoftFloat mutations. It ignores rounding control, applies DAZ before conversion, raises precision rather than denormal for nonzero fractional input and returns masked integer indefinite for invalid conversion. The truncated signed magnitude determines range: negative fractional inputs slightly beyond -2^31 can still produce valid -2^31 with precision status. Invalid conversion suppresses precision status. The helper records current status and stops before the emitted GPR assignment on an unmasked exception. All vector bits and integer flags are preserved.

An independent rational/integer-division model agrees with 212,480 canonical W0 register and unaligned-memory Rosetta conversions: 106,240 numerical/control candidates from 1,660 inputs, all sixteen rounding/DAZ/FTZ combinations and four sticky-status patterns. Inputs include signed32 limit ULP neighborhoods, one-unit overflow boundaries, selected exponent/mantissa boundaries, both NaN classes/signs, infinities, zeros, denormals and deterministic random patterns. Canonical checks retain the full source YMM, selected integer flags and guarded source memory. W1 IA-32 behavior is checked natively against the SDM contract; the 64-bit Rosetta reference uses W0.

Actual emitted ARM bodies pass 591,872 cases across 289 blocks on macOS, iPad Pro M5 then iPhone 17 Pro, one simulator at a time. Eleven strict negative fixtures pass. An initial numerical schedule passed but covered only 70,336 candidates; the final schedule was revised and rerun on all three profiles, covering every 106,240 independent candidate at least once per profile. This is not every candidate at every register/address role.

A further 128 emitted boundary probes per profile cover all GPR destinations and REG/MEM forms: unmasked invalid/signaling-NaN and precision/subnormal faults preserve full CPU and guarded memory except current sticky status. Masked invalid with precision unmasked, masked precision with invalid unmasked, DAZ and exact conversion with all masks clear verify that prior sticky bits do not trigger a new fault. The current unserviced boundary is SIGABRT; precise Windows fault delivery and physical Intel execution remain unproven. ### Scalar minimum, maximum and ordered double comparison verified

VMINSS, VMAXSS and VCOMISD now decode and lower for finite C5/C4 WIG L0 register and scalar-memory forms. Masked MIN/MAX results merge the first source's upper 96 bits and clear the destination's upper YMM half. ### Relocated import-slot comparisons and pushes verified

The strict translator now handles the original dbghelp IAT accesses that blocked whole-DLL translation: the dword immediate comparison at 0x10053C97 and pushes at 0x10053D92 and 0x10053D9F. This finite extension requires Tribes full-flags mode, flat IA-32 absolute dword memory, one relocation and CMP-immediate or PUSH. Both disassembly stages retain the import declaration for resolution while treating the operand as guest IAT data. The production VA transformation maps its relocated label to the original guest address, so emitted ARM reads the slot's current four-byte value. SRW's raw import sections remain omitted from its data output; the original PE memory mapping supplies those bytes. Existing MOV/direct CALL/JMP import paths remain as previously implemented. Microsoft documents resolved IAT entries as symbol addresses in the [PE format contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/debug/pe-format); [StackWalk64](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/dbghelp/nf-dbghelp-stackwalk64) accepts SymFunctionTableAccess64 and SymGetModuleBase64 callbacks. Those facts explain the original pointer comparisons and pushes; this change does not implement dbghelp or invoke those callbacks.

Final emitted ARM tests pass 1,048,576 cases across sixteen bodies per platform on macOS, iPhone 17 Pro then iPad Pro M5, one simulator at a time. The fixture retains all three original instruction byte sequences, the five original dbghelp import names/slot addresses and real import descriptors plus external relocation records. Synthetic dword CMP forms cover signed eight-bit and full 32-bit immediates. Mutable slots include zero, import-token, boundary and random values. Independent integer expectations compare every defined subtraction flag, all 440 CPU bytes, every MXCSR value and 97 guarded memory bytes. PUSH checks the exact ESP decrement and four-byte stack write, including source/destination overlap. The final build also checks that imports read as data remain declared. Three unsupported fixtures fail explicitly. A separate 65,536-case x86 Unicorn reference agrees with independently computed flags and stack effects and checks all eight GPR/YMM registers, flags, MXCSR and guarded memory. This is a software reference with a separate corpus, not physical Intel proof.

The first combined fixture used a stale comprehension variable for relocation targets; the target validation rejected it before native compilation. After correcting the generator, testing exposed that SRW omits raw import-section data labels; the test now uses the same original-address transformation as production rather than claiming those raw sections link directly. Import declaration bookkeeping was subsequently added and the final native suite was rerun on all three profiles. Runtime helper sources are unchanged. Prior full native suites are retained, not rerun under this compiler; fresh move-decoder and third-source relocation frontend regressions pass.

### Packed unsigned byte absolute-difference sums verified

VPSADBW now decodes and lowers for finite C5/C4 WIG 128/256-bit register and unaligned memory forms. Each group of eight unsigned byte differences is summed into the low sixteen bits of a 64-bit result lane; other result bits are zero. Inputs are snapshotted before publication, retaining source/destination aliases. The 128-bit form clears upper YMM, and flags/MXCSR remain unchanged. The 256-bit form requires AVX2; full AVX2 CPUID advertisement is still unfinished. Primary contract: [Intel SDM revision 089](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/868137/325462-089-sdm-vol-1-2abcd-3abcd-4.pdf), local zero-based PDF pages 1679–1682. Unsupported maps, selectors, extended roles, legacy mandatory prefixes, segments and address size fail explicitly.

Actual emitted ARM bodies pass 2,361,344 cases across 4,612 blocks on macOS, iPad Pro M5 then iPhone 17 Pro, one simulator at a time. The fixture preserves all four original audited DLL instruction bytes, and covers both widths, every source/destination alias, C5/C4 WIG and GPR/SIB/stack/absolute memory addressing. Independent signed-difference sums compare results and required zero bits against the unsigned helper implementation. A coverage bitmap proves all 65,536 unsigned byte input pairs occur in the union of inputs per native profile; this does not claim every pair at every register/address role or width. Distinct group extremes and random mixed bytes supplement the exhaustive pairs. Full 440-byte CPU, all MXCSR values and 97-byte guarded memory are checked. Eight strict negative fixtures and fresh SAD/prior move-decoder checks pass.

A separate canonical Rosetta reference agrees with Python byte sums for 266,240 cases: every 65,536 repeated-byte pair plus 1,024 mixed random groups for each of four 128/256 REG/MEM modes. It compares three full YMM registers, selected flags, masked MXCSR controls and unaligned source memory. This corpus is separate from the emitted native alias tests, and Rosetta is a software reference; physical Intel execution and precise Windows fault delivery are not proven.

The full community DLL passes the first SAD and now stops at VPMADDUBSW, 0x100C5BD7, bytes c4e27d04c7, `vpmaddubsw ymm0, ymm0, ymm7`. Its following original instructions include VPMADDWD and VPADDD; they form the next related multiply-add/add pipeline. The bound static inventory now finds 2,775 decoded and 207 unsupported VEX sites across 28 families among 2,982 recursively audited unprefixed starts. Static decoding is not backend/runtime/gameplay proof.

### Packed multiply-add and wraparound additions verified

VPMADDUBSW, VPMADDWD, VPADDD and VPADDB now decode and lower for finite WIG 128/256-bit register and unaligned memory forms. Byte multiply-add uses unsigned first-source bytes and signed second-source bytes, adding adjacent products with signed-word saturation. Word multiply-add sums signed products in a private 64-bit value before publishing the low 32 bits, including the all-8000H input case that wraps to 80000000H. Byte and dword additions wrap independently at their respective widths. Helpers snapshot aliases and publish a complete result; 128-bit destinations clear upper YMM. Integer flags and MXCSR are preserved. MADDUBSW uses the C4 0F38 map; the other three use C5/C4 0F. Unsupported selectors/maps, extended roles, prefixes, segments and address size fail explicitly. Primary contract: [Intel SDM revision 089](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/868137/325462-089-sdm-vol-1-2abcd-3abcd-4.pdf), local packed-add section beginning at zero-based PDF page 1471, MADDUBSW 1571 and MADDWD 1574. Full AVX2 CPUID advertisement remains unfinished.

Final emitted ARM tests pass 4,335,360 cases across 16,935 bodies per platform on macOS, iPhone 17 Pro then iPad Pro M5, one simulator at a time. Coverage includes all role aliases, both widths, C5/C4 W0/W1 where applicable, GPR/SIB/stack/absolute addressing, signed integer boundaries, word multiply overflow, byte/dword carry truncation and random mixed lanes. Exact unsaturated sums around ±32768 and both saturation extremes were added after the initial macOS pass; the final suite was rerun on all three profiles. Independent widened-integer expectations check all 440 CPU bytes, every MXCSR value and 97-byte guarded memory. Eight strict negative fixtures and fresh multiply/add, SAD and prior move-decoder tests pass. A decoder harness compilation failure caused by commas inside an `assert` compound literal was corrected before its successful run.

It checks all 65,536 unsigned/signed byte products and byte-add input pairs per applicable mode, exact signed-saturation neighborhoods, 1,296 signed-word corner quadruples, 144 dword-add boundary pairs and 4,096 mixed random groups per mode. Three full YMM registers, flags, masked MXCSR controls and source memory are compared. Native and canonical corpora are separate; physical Intel execution and precise Windows exception delivery are not established.

### Packed shifts verified

VPSLLD, VPSRLQ, VPSLLDQ, VPSRLDQ and VPSLLVD now lower to native ARM helpers. Scalar shifts consume the full low 64-bit count; counts at or above the element width produce zero. Variable dword shifts use a separate unsigned count for each element. Byte shifts operate independently within each 128-bit lane. The helpers snapshot all inputs before publishing, preserve integer flags and MXCSR, and clear upper YMM for 128-bit destinations. Scalar memory counts read exactly 16 bytes even with a 256-bit destination; variable counts read 16 or 32 bytes according to width. The decoder distinguishes WIG scalar/immediate forms from the W0 variable form and immediate destinations encoded in VEX.vvvv.

Emitted ARM tests pass 3,934,976 cases across 30,742 bodies per profile on macOS, iPhone 17 Pro and iPad Pro M5, one simulator at a time. All 22 original audited shift sites are included. Coverage checks all register aliases, both widths, all 256 immediate values on canonical roles, boundary counts on all roles, scalar high-count bits, per-element unsigned counts, lane boundaries, exact guarded memory, all 440 CPU bytes and every MXCSR value. Ten unsupported fixtures fail explicitly. Fresh shift, prior multiply/add and small-move decoder checks pass. A separate canonical Rosetta reference agrees with independent Python integer expectations for 90,112 cases across 20 modes; this is not physical Intel evidence or a case-for-case comparison to the native corpus.

### Variable byte blend verified

VPBLENDVB now decodes and lowers through finite IA-32 C4 W0 66 0F3A opcode 4C at both vector widths. Each mask byte's high bit chooses the right source byte; otherwise the left byte is copied. Lower seven mask bits are ignored. IA-32 selects the mask register from encoded /is4 bits 6:4; bit 7 and the low nibble are ignored. All inputs are captured before publication, preserving every operand alias, flags and MXCSR; 128-bit results clear upper YMM and memory right sources read exactly 16/32 bytes. Unsupported W/selector/map/extended roles/segment/address size/mandatory prefix forms fail explicitly. Primary contract: [Intel SDM instruction reference](https://www.intel.com/content/dam/www/public/us/en/documents/manuals/64-ia-32-architectures-software-developer-vol-2b-manual.pdf), VPBLENDVB. Full AVX2 CPUID support remains unfinished.

Emitted ARM tests pass 3,801,344 cases across 14,849 bodies per profile on macOS, iPhone 17 Pro then iPad Pro M5, one simulator at a time. The original audited instruction is retained. Coverage includes every four-register-role alias combination, both widths, four memory-address forms and all 256 encoded /is4 bytes at canonical register/memory roles. A bitmap verifies all mask-byte values at every output position across each profile. Independent byte conditionals compare all 440 CPU bytes, every MXCSR value and guarded unaligned memory. Nine negative fixtures and fresh blend, integer-extraction and broad prior decoder checks pass. A separate canonical Rosetta x86-64 reference agrees with Python selections for 65,536 cases across both widths and register/memory right operands, including mixed mask lanes, destination/left alias, three full YMM registers, flags, masked MXCSR and memory. This reference does not directly execute IA-32's ignored /is4 bit 7; native and decoder tests check that encoding against the Intel contract. Physical Intel/full initialization/gameplay are not established.

### Integer vector insertion verified

VINSERTI128 now decodes and lowers through finite IA-32 C4 W0/L1/66 0F3A opcode 38. Its low immediate bit selects the destination's 128-bit half; other immediate bits are ignored. The other half comes from the 256-bit merge input. Helpers capture the complete merge and 128-bit inserted input before publication, preserving register aliases, flags and MXCSR. Memory inputs read exactly 16 bytes. Unsupported W/width/selector/map/extended-role/segment/address-size/mandatory-prefix forms fail explicitly. ### Scalar BMI2 shifts verified; VEX audit expanded

SHLX and SHRX now decode and lower through finite IA-32 C4 0F38 opcode F7, L0, with 66/F2 selecting left/right logical shifts. W is ignored in IA-32. Each instruction masks its general-register count to five bits, reads the source before writing the destination and preserves every flag. Register aliases are retained, and memory sources read exactly four bytes. Unsupported widths/selectors/maps/extended roles/segments/address size/mandatory prefixes fail explicitly. ### Byte-sign mask extraction verified

VPMOVMSKB now decodes and lowers through finite IA-32 C5/C4 WIG 66 0F opcode D7 with reserved vvvv. Each source byte's high bit becomes the corresponding GPR result bit. ### Embedded compression dispatch established; native buffer integration verified

The original DLL identifies its embedded library as zlib-ng 2.2.3. The pinned upstream tag is commit cbb6ec1d74e8061efdf7251f8c2dae778bed14fd, downloaded into ref/zlib-ng with its license and recorded in dependencies.lock.json. Its 15-field x86 feature layout and 15-slot function table agree with the examined binary. This is a matching source reference, not a reproducible-build proof of the DLL. Source: [zlib-ng 2.2.3](https://github.com/zlib-ng/zlib-ng/tree/2.2.3).

The original feature initializer at 0x100C1B60 and dispatcher at 0x100BDB40 were executed in Unicorn using the actual native guest_cpu.c CPUID helper. Five profiles cover native, diagnostic SSE2, AVX2, AVX-512 without ZMM OS state, and AVX-512 with OS state. Two initial publications per profile agree. The current native profile selects scalar Adler entries 0x100C1C20 and 0x100C1BF0. The diagnostic AVX-512 profile selects wrappers 0x100C74E0/0x100C7500, which call the routine containing the first unsupported EVEX site; withholding ZMM OS state retains the AVX2 entries. The detector requires AVX512F/DQ/BW/VL, BMI2 and XCR0 state. The shipped source's BMI2 mask is retained as evidence rather than silently corrected. This establishes this dispatcher’s behavior, not global indirect unreachability of every EVEX routine.

The native-profile original lazy-dispatch entries also execute 160 checksum/copy cases against Python zlib, with original source/destination guards and callee-saved registers checked. A native ARM64 zlib-ng static library now builds for macOS, iOS device and simulator. The new port/core/guest_zlib.c adapter translates nonnull guest buffer offsets, rejects guest/host arithmetic overflow and overlapping copies, and calls native Adler/checksum-copy implementations. It does not reinterpret 32-bit guest stream structures, callbacks or allocators. Invalid mappings and guest fault delivery remain runtime responsibilities; null-buffer behavior is currently an explicit unsupported boundary. The adapter is compiled on all three targets but is not yet wired into module dispatch or the complete client link.

Native buffer tests match those 160 original-DLL results at 16 alignments, with entire arena comparison: 2,560 cases on macOS, then iPad Pro M5, then iPhone 17 Pro, one simulator booted at a time. Four macOS negative processes verify strict guest-range overflow, host-pointer overflow, null and overlap rejection. The iOS device result is compilation only. Frozen source-bound evidence: generated/zlib-ng/checkpoint-20261001. The latest status-only turn was no progress; this turn adds the native adapter, builds and execution evidence.

### Native optimized Adler integration advances strict DLL translation

The previous goal turn was progress: original-PC scalar cdecl routes were implemented and tested. This turn implements the distinct five-argument optimized Adler core contract at 0x100C7520. ### Exact fixed32 chunk-copy integration advances the next DLL boundary

The previous goal turn was progress: optimized Adler integration passed its native checks and strict translation advanced to BZHI0x100C7752. Binary/source inspection identifies that site inside the fixed32 chunk-copy helper0x100C7740..0x100C77B7. It snapshots a complete32-byte source chunk before each store, advances the first chunk by length modulo32 (or32), then copies full chunks. Short copies use masked reads/stores. This ordering matters for overlapping buffers; replacing the helper with a single memcpy or memmove would change some outputs. The new port/core/guest_zlib_chunk.c preserves the original chunk order with a temporary32-byte snapshot, accepts zero-length NULL buffers without access, returns the guest output pointer plus length and clears all finite YMM upper halves. Caller-saved lower-register clobbers are not replayed; this is a native cdecl function boundary. Reference: [pinned zlib-ng chunkset_avx512.c](https://github.com/zlib-ng/zlib-ng/blob/2.2.3/arch/x86/chunkset_avx512.c), with its zlib license retained in ref/zlib-ng/LICENSE.md.

It verifies1,249 cases: lengths0..80 and eight larger boundaries through1,024, 14 overlap distances in both directions including identical pointers, and zero-length NULL combinations. Output bytes, requested-write boundaries, callee-saved registers, argument/return stack and upper clearing are checked. This routine has no substituted callees. It remains an offline vector model, not hardware AVX512 evidence. Extending the model also justified rerunning the535-case optimized Adler reference; those results remain consistent, including its explicit scalar-tail substitution limitation.

Earlier whole instruction/ABI suites are retained and not claimed as freshly rerun.

### Required fences lower to explicit ARM64 barriers

The previous turn verified legacy word extraction. LFENCE/MFENCE/SFENCE now have flags metadata and native lowering rather than an ignored instruction. All three execute DSB SY with compiler memory clobbers; LFENCE additionally executes ISB for execution synchronization. This conservative mapping completes both loads and stores, intentionally stronger than the minimum LFENCE/SFENCE memory ordering, with potential performance cost. It follows Intel's LFENCE execution-ordering description and Arm's barrier/speculation guidance; source URLs and limitations are pinned in generated/sse/fence-contract.json. Lock/repeat prefixes fail explicitly. No guest CPU/vector bytes are modified.

The original LFENCE at0x100013E2 and24 finite register-ModRM aliases produce25 emitted bodies. Runs were macOS, then iPhone17 Pro Simulator, then iPad Pro M5 Simulator, one simulator at a time. Compiled ARM assembly is checked for DSB SY and ISB, and three unsupported prefix fixtures fail. The concurrent test uses relaxed atomic data with independent phase/completion synchronization, avoiding data races. Its empirical outcome is not exhaustive memory-model/speculation proof, complete guest multithreading or actual game execution. Earlier arithmetic suites remain retained evidence; decoder sources are unchanged. Native Win32 runtime objects compile sequentially for macOS, iOS device and simulator.

### SHLD full-flags implementation in progress

Appended TB_SHLD enum value5 preserves all previous operation IDs. The32-bit shift core masks count to five bits, leaves value/flags unchanged for zero count, shifts source high bits into the low destination and calculates CF/SF/ZF/PF. For a one-bit shift it calculates OF from the sign change; nonzero AF and multi-bit OF are undefined and deliberately retain their old values. Existing shift wrappers handle operand/count snapshots. Full-flags SRW lowering now routes32-bit SHLD register or memory destinations with register source and IMM8/CL count through those wrappers. Unsupported SHLD address16/segment/LOCK/16bit forms fail in this finite path. Optimized SHLD lowering is not claimed fixed. ### Region/data repair completes the selected DLL diagnostic census — 2 October 2026

Noninstruction outputs, labels and relocations remain untouched for the data writer. tests in scripts/test-lifter-regions.py execute the actual partition function under AddressSanitizer/UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer with independently specified code/data/gap boundaries. Strict unsupported packed shift and scalar compare forms still fail; only diagnostic mode replaces them with explicit traps. The unsupported SIMD CMPSD case now returns the existing unsupported-form code8, enabling the census without treating relocation errors as recoverable.

### Original community DLL now enters natively; loader provider is the next boundary — 2 October 2026

### Native providers resolve; process attach rejects subpage protection — 2 October 2026

Added manifest-generated finite system providers for kernel32/kernelbase (27 exports each) and ntdll (one export), with immutable dispatch to existing ARM64 wrappers. Provider identities avoid DirectInput and the VM allocator; the ntdll metadata handle is 0x20000000. Tests check all55 export lookups, initial/load/free references, missing exports, DirectInput coexistence, and64 dynamically resolved TLS cycles with complete CPU/stack preservation. This is a bounded native service provider, not an entire Windows DLL implementation. Evidence: generated/integration-review/system-providers/result.json.

Added an exact native VirtualProtect data-page implementation in guest_address_space.c and its generated four-argument stdcall binding. It validates a committed single allocation and writable old-protection output, enforces native read-only/no-access changes, preserves LastError on success, and rejects unsupported subpage geometry, execution/flags and dynamic owners without mutation. The host tests verify real write/read faults, unchanged neighbors, byte-range rounding, output guards and complete caller CPU preservation. Existing VirtualQuery normal and ThreadSanitizer regressions pass. Evidence: generated/native-win32/virtual-protect/macos/result.json and generated/native-win32/virtual-query-abi/macos/result.json. Independent4 KiB protection and precise Windows fault delivery remain unimplemented.

The native IFC22 diagnostic now declares its actual RW image/stack/TLS mappings and records each protection result. Original TLS returns. Process attach requests PAGE_READONLY for128 bytes at0x10475000, sharing the native16 KiB page with writable data. VirtualProtect correctly returns0/ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED(50) under this implementation, and the original DLL then returnsFALSE. The host diagnostic exit0 is explicitly classified initialized=false, outcome=attach_failed. This is a required boundary; continuing after a failed attach is not permitted. Initial diagnostic images still lack original per-section permissions. The original module object is unchanged (SHA c6104e8c6ccf74a3be2dbeed46ab3fb2ee38272253f0cda8f81f2a7cdb816e6a). Next investigate verified AOT memory-access checks for guest subpages, including native helper/library buffer accesses; do not replace protection with a success-returning stub. Renderer, controls connected to actual gameplay, HD rendering and authenticated multiplayer remain incomplete. No simulator was run in this batch; goal active.

The base-engine native/original-x86 startup regression passes again with the provider and VirtualProtect changes; first_entry_differential_pass=true and game_startup_completed=false. The original IFC22 haptic import remains its separate stop. The 26-file source/build/native-binary checkpoint is generated/integration-review/protection-checkpoint-20261002.


### AOT memory-check foundation executes the original startup prefix — 2 October 2026

Added port/compiler/GuestMemoryPass.cpp and a separate trusted guest_memory_checks runtime. The LLVM20.1.8 build-time pass brackets scalar/vector loads and stores, atomic operations, memory intrinsics, ordinary/fortified memcpy/memmove/memset and Darwin symbol aliases. Permission publications serialize with bracketed accesses through a read/write lock. It removes stale function/call memory and synchronization attributes, rejects uncovered memory operations and records external call targets for the link audit. It is applied after optimization with O0 code generation afterward. Objective-C ARC contraction runs before instrumentation. This creates precompiled ARM64 code; it does not generate executable code at runtime.

Actual emitted fixtures pass23 isolated forbidden-access faults with all64 KiB of shared memory unchanged, including cross-page stores/copies, vector accesses, atomics and fortified library calls. Allowed neighboring/host/zero-byte operations and permission restoration pass. Both normal and ThreadSanitizer runs complete8,000 concurrent emitted write/read/copy cycles and4,000 concurrent permission publications; the fault subprocesses run in the normal build only. Duplicate instrumentation, scalable accesses, masked loads and va_arg are rejected. Evidence: generated/integration-review/guest-memory-pass/result.json. Fault delivery is an explicit diagnostic abort, not Windows SEH.

The actual diagnostic DLL receives checks around374,922 loads and288,423 stores; its separate checked object is57,367,976 bytes, SHA381bc1266780164e54c8be9a026e42e292cda9eca4afb2c8405cb19798c1273e. It retains637 external call targets requiring a link audit. All62 native service objects compile with4,470 loads,2,288 stores,384 transfers,99 sets and one atomic operation instrumented. Source-bound reports are in native-diagnostic/checked/result.json and generated/native-win32/checked-macos/result.json. Successful compilation is not complete memory coverage: the observed ABI wrapper, dispatch/support sources and math/zlib/platform/library boundaries still need integration or explicit contracts.

scripts/test-qol-native-bootstrap.py --checked-runtime links the checked image and checked native service objects, configures an unrestricted software shadow alongside the existing native protection model, and executes actual TLS/process attach. It reproduces the same failed VirtualProtect at0x10475000 and FALSE process-attach return. link_coverage_complete=false and independent_subpages_enabled=false are explicit report fields. This proves the reached prefix still executes with instrumentation; it does not prove complete semantic equivalence or successful initialization. Next complete helper/library coverage, connect synchronized software subpage publication to VirtualProtect, then immediately retry original initialization before optional instruction work. No new simulator run or gameplay acceptance is claimed; goal active.

NTSTATUS source: [Wine10.0 conversion](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/wine-10.0/dlls/ntdll/error.c), [mapping table](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/wine-10.0/dlls/ntdll/error.h), and [x86 TEB layout](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/wine-10.0/include/winternl.h). Exact inputs are in `config/runtime/ntstatus-sources.json`; the retained license is `port/licenses/Wine-LGPL-2.1.txt`.
